<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For each fixed Kerr spacetime with mass <i>M</i> &gt; 0 and rotation $`0\lt \mathfrak a\lt M`$, we prove that a dense <i>G</i><sub><i>δ</i></sub> set of nearby smooth, complete two-ended vacuum data has a maximal globally hyperbolic development with no future <i>C</i><sup>1</sup> extension. The neighborhood and genericity are defined in a weighted smooth topology, and ambient extensions may be nonvacuum.

- **claim-generic-future-inextendibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-phase-main)): For each fixed $M>0$ and $0<\mathfrak a<M$, there is $\varepsilon_0=\varepsilon_0(M,\mathfrak a)>0$ such that $\mathcal U_{\varepsilon_0}$ contains a dense $G_\delta$ subset $\mathcal G$ for which every $\mathcal M_d$, $d\in\mathcal G$, has no future $C^1$ extension in the sense of Definition <a href="#phase:extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="phase:extension">2</a>.
  - Explanation: A residual subset of a sufficiently small weighted smooth neighborhood of each fixed rotating subextremal Kerr bridge has full maximal developments admitting no future extension of the specified regularity. The conclusion permits nonvacuum ambient extensions.
- **claim-baire-constraint-space** (theorem, [source](#stmt-phase-baire)): The space $\mathcal D$ is completely metrizable, and every $\mathcal U_\varepsilon$ is a nonempty Baire space. At any point, finite seminorm balls form a neighborhood base: every neighborhood contains a set of the form $$\{d'\in\mathcal D:p_m(d'-d)<r\}$$ for some finite $m\ge10$ and $r>0$.
  - Explanation: Complete metrizability supplies the category argument, while finite seminorm balls allow each density experiment to address an arbitrary neighborhood.
- **claim-observer-positive-metric-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-test-observer-comparison)): Let $U=\dot\gamma_{d,w}$ and set $r_U=g_d+2U^\flat\otimes U^\flat$. This positive metric is parallel along $\gamma_{d,w}$ and satisfies $$\label{test:observer-norm-bound} c(w)^{-1}k_{d,w}\le r_U\le c(w)k_{d,w}, \qquad c(w)=\bigl(\sqrt{1+h(w,w)}+\sqrt{h(w,w)}\bigr)^2.$$ In particular the comparison is uniform when the initial data and seeds range in a sufficiently small neighborhood of a fixed pair.
  - Explanation: The observer's positive metric and the transported initial positive metric remain comparable along the geodesic. Their comparison is uniform near a fixed datum and seed.
- **claim-transport-tests-closed** (theorem, [source](#stmt-test-closed)): Each $\mathcal A(O,B)$ is relatively closed in $\mathcal U$.
  - Explanation: A failure of the lifetime or transport requirement has a compact witness whose strict inequalities persist under nearby smooth Cauchy evolution.
- **claim-causal-homotopy-diamond** (theorem, [source](#stmt-test-diamond)): Let $P$ be a smooth globally hyperbolic open subspacetime of a Hausdorff manifold $\widetilde M$ carrying a continuous Lorentzian metric. Let $H:[0,1]^2\to\widetilde M$ be continuous, with $H(\lambda,0)=p$ and $H(\lambda,1)=q$ for fixed $p,q\in P$. Suppose every $H(\lambda,\cdot)$ is future causal. If the path for $\lambda=0$ lies in $P$, then all the paths lie in $P$.
  - Explanation: An intrinsic compact causal diamond prevents a fixed-endpoint causal homotopy from leaving the globally hyperbolic open subspacetime.
- **claim-no-interior-geodesic-endpoint** (theorem, [source](#stmt-test-interior-endpoint)): A maximal timelike geodesic in a smooth spacetime has no endpoint there as an unparametrized causal curve. Its maximal past continuation from a point in $I^+(\Sigma)$ therefore crosses a Cauchy hypersurface $\Sigma$.
  - Explanation: An interior position limit also gives a limiting timelike tangent and smooth continuation. Maximal past continuation from the future of a Cauchy hypersurface must therefore cross it.
- **claim-small-developed-length-bootstrap** (bound, [source](#stmt-test-loop-bootstrap)): Consider a family of base points in regular coordinate charts for a $C^1$ Lorentzian metric. Suppose the Christoffel symbols are bounded by one constant, each base point has coordinate distance at least $\rho>0$ from its chart boundary, and the coordinate matrices of the prescribed positive orthonormal frames and their inverses have norm at most $K$. There are $l_*>0$ and $C_*<\infty$, depending only on these constants, such that every piecewise smooth loop based at any of these points with developed length $L<l_*$ remains in its selected chart and satisfies $$\label{test:local-loop-bound} \lVert P_\ell-\operatorname{Id}\rVert_k\le C_*L.$$ The loop may be specified in any open subspacetime of the chart’s ambient manifold; no initial assumption of coordinate smallness is required.
  - Explanation: Bounded connection coefficients, chart margins and initial frame bounds control arbitrary loops by developed length. A first-exit argument establishes chart containment before applying the transport estimate.
- **claim-future-exits-covered-by-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-test-exit-cover)): If $\mathcal M_d$, $d\in\mathcal U$, admits a future $C^1$ extension in the stated sense, then $d\in\mathcal A(O,B)$ for some $O\in\mathcal O$ and some integer $B\ge1$.
  - Explanation: Any future extension supplies an open family of finite-duration intrinsic geodesics with a common short-loop transport bound, placing the datum in a passing test.
- **claim-comparison-development-runout** (classification, [source](#stmt-test-comparison)): After decreasing the defining $p_{10}$ radius of $\mathcal U$, each $d\in\mathcal U$ has a smooth globally hyperbolic vacuum comparison development $\mathcal P_d$ containing the whole bridge $\Sigma$ as a Cauchy hypersurface and embedded openly and isometrically in $\mathcal M_d$. It contains a spacelike cylinder $\mathcal S$ and its full future product wedge, with coordinates $t=(u+v)/2\ge0$, $x=(v-u)/2\in\mathbb R$, $\theta\in\mathbb S^2$, in which $$\label{test:wedge} \begin{split} g&=-2a\,du\,dv+ \gamma_{AB}(d\theta^A-b^Adu)(d\theta^B-b^Bdu),\\ a&=q\mathcal A,\qquad q=q_0e^{-\kappa(u+v)},\qquad q_0,\kappa>0. \end{split}$$ The coefficients $\mathcal A^{\pm1}$ and $\gamma^{\pm1}$ are uniformly bounded, with $\gamma$ uniformly comparable to a fixed round sphere metric. Every seed with $T_d(w)<\infty$ crosses $\mathcal S$ before its first runout from $\mathcal P_d$, and that first runout has exactly one of the following coordinate limits: $$\label{test:runout} \begin{array}{lll} \text{first branch:}&u\to u_*<\infty,&v\to+\infty,\\ \text{second branch:}&v\to v_*<\infty,&u\to+\infty,\\ \text{corner:}&u\to+\infty,&v\to+\infty. \end{array}$$ These alternatives describe limiting coordinate regimes; they do not adjoin boundary points or identify $\mathcal P_d$ with $\mathcal M_d$.
  - Explanation: The comparison development contains the complete initial bridge and a full optical wedge. Finite-duration seeds first run out through a branch or the corner, as coordinate limits rather than adjoined boundary points.
- **claim-individual-wedge-track-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-test-corner-bounds)): For a future unit timelike geodesic beginning at a fixed point of the wedge, set $Y_i=e^{-\kappa y_i}$ and $\Xi=\dot\theta-b\dot u$. On its remaining wedge track each $\|\dot Y_i\|$ is bounded above and below by positive constants, and $\|\Xi\|_\gamma$ is bounded. The constants may depend on its initial unit tangent. In particular every corner track has one finite bound for both $\dot Y_i$ and $\Xi$ throughout a sufficiently late tail.
  - Explanation: Optical decay velocities and angular screen velocity are controlled along each individual timelike track. The constants depend on its initial tangent and need not be common to an open family.
- **claim-corner-seeds-null** (theorem, [source](#stmt-test-corner-null)): For a fixed $d\in\mathcal U$, the seeds whose first runout from $\mathcal P_d$ is a corner are contained in a set of measure zero for every smooth positive measure on $T\Sigma$.
  - Explanation: Canonical volume preservation and shrinking null-section strips place all corner seeds inside a null set for any smooth positive seed measure.
- **claim-passing-test-branch-seed** (theorem, [source](#stmt-test-branch-seed)): If $d\in\mathcal A(O,B)$, then there is $w\in O$ whose first runout from $\mathcal P_d$ is one of the two branches in Equation <a href="#test:runout" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="test:runout">[test:runout]</a>. After exchanging the optical labels and preparing the angular coordinates as in (OpenAI 2026b, companion@kind@foundation@in:label-swap companion@kind@foundation@in:label-swap ) [[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)], the branch can be written $u\to u_*<\infty$, $v\to\infty$. The seed is still the same initial seed in $O$.
  - Explanation: A nonempty open seed set cannot consist entirely of corner seeds. Exchanging the optical preparation selects the standard branch convention without changing the observer or its initial seed.
- **claim-all-spin-geometric-interface** (bound, [source](#stmt-bg-geometry)): There is a choice of $\epsilon>0$, depending only on the fixed Kerr center and the fixed preparation, such that every $p_{10}(d-d_*)<\epsilon$ in the stated smooth symbol class has the following properties.* 1. *The cylinder $\mathcal S$ in Proposition <a href="#test:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:comparison">12</a> has a smooth vacuum development on the full product wedge $[0,\infty)_t\times\mathbb R_x\times\mathbb S^2$ in Equation <a href="#bg:wedge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bg:wedge">[bg:wedge]</a>. The quantities $\mathcal A^{\pm1},\gamma^{\pm1}$ are uniformly bounded in angular $H^6$, and $\xi_i,a^{-1}\partial_vb$ in angular $H^5$. There are nonnegative functions $g_i(y_i)$ with summable unit-cell suprema such that, in the indicated low angular norms, $$\label{bg:envelopes} \\|\chi_i,\ell_i+\kappa\\|_{H^4}\le C(q+g_i(y_i)), \qquad \\|b\\|_{H^5}\le C(q+g_0(u)).$$* 2. *The following quantities are uniformly bounded, and tend to zero when $t,\|u\|,\|v\|\to\infty$: $$\label{bg:low-limits} \begin{gathered} \\|b\\|_{H^5}+\sum_i\\|\chi_i,\ell_i+\kappa\\|_{H^4},\\ \sum_{k=0}^1\left(\\|\partial_{y_k}b\\|_{H^2} +\sum_i\\|\partial_{y_k}\chi_i, \partial_{y_k}\ell_i\\|_{H^2}\right),\\ \sum_{k=0}^1\left(\\|\partial_{y_k}\gamma\\|_{H^4} +\\|\partial_{y_k}\log a+\kappa\\|_{H^4}\right). \end{gathered}$$ In particular $D\log a,D\gamma,b,Db$ are uniformly bounded.* 3. *For each fixed $U\in\mathbb R$, $C>0$, and finite order, all ordinary derivatives of $$\label{bg:high-arrays} \gamma^{\pm1},\quad\log\mathcal A,\quad b, \quad a^{-1}\partial_vb$$ are bounded by $e^{o(T)}$ on $$\label{bg:truncation} t\ge0,\qquad y_d\le U,\qquad y_l\le CT, \qquad \{d,l\}=\{0,1\}.$$ This includes every restriction and coordinate-path subsegment. Every fixed derivative of $a$, $\sqrt a$, or $\partial_vb$ retains, respectively, the factor $a$, $\sqrt a$, or $a$.* 4. *Either branch can be put in the convention $v\to\infty$, $u\to u_*<\infty$. Exchanging the optical labels and preparing the angular coordinates anew preserves all these conclusions on the same product spacetime and initial cylinder.* *Only $p_{10}$ is required small; no higher seminorm, radiation coefficient, or curvature measured by a timelike observer is required small or bounded.
  - Explanation: Smallness in the defining weighted seminorm provides a full wedge, low geometric control and subexponential fixed-order jets on expanding truncated regions. Higher background seminorms need only be finite.
- **claim-branch-observer-normalization** (bound, [source](#stmt-bg-branch)): Let a future unit timelike geodesic approach a branch $v\to\infty$, $u\to u_*<\infty$, in the comparison wedge. With proper time denoted by $\tau$, put $$p=a\dot v,\qquad \Xi=\dot\theta-b\dot u,\qquad P=\gamma\Xi.$$ Then $p\asymp1$, and $P$, $\Xi$, and $\dot u$ stay bounded on a late segment. The angular position has a limit. At the point $p_T$ with $v=T$ one has $$\label{bg:branch-scales} a_p:=a(p_T)\asymp e^{-\kappa T}, \qquad \dot v(p_T)\asymp e^{\kappa T}.$$
  - Explanation: Low geometric estimates normalize an arbitrary branch observer, bounding angular momentum and the finite optical velocity while identifying lapse decay and growth of the divergent optical velocity.
- **claim-exterior-entry-escape-flow** (bound, [source](#stmt-bg-exterior)): On either prepared end use regular exterior time $t_e$, co-moving coordinates $y$, and lab coordinates $z=y+C_e(t_e)$, with fixed $\sup\|C_e'\|\le s_*<1/10$, after decreasing $\epsilon$ if needed. The relevant slice $t_e=0$ agrees with the original bridge sufficiently far out. There is a preparation parameter $I$ with $0<c_I\le I'\le C_I$ and $I(t_T)=T$. The launch patch near $v=x=T$ on $\mathcal S$ occurs at $t_e=t_T+O(1)$, and $t_T\asymp T$. A fixed enlargement is contained in $t_e\le t_T+C_2$. Its selected-end portion $v<(1-\nu_0)T$ is earlier by at least $c\nu_0T-O(1)$ for each fixed $\nu_0>0$.* *The exterior metric has uniformly regular spacelike temporal levels. Its first and second ordinary lab derivatives are bounded on the computing region and become arbitrarily small at sufficiently large co-radius, uniformly in time. Every fixed mixed jet on the used time and radius ranges $O(1+T)$ is $e^{o(T)}$. Entry charts have a noncharacteristic collar with controlled low jets and subexponential fixed higher jets; the inner exit has a strict margin deeper than the entry cylinder.* *The null covectors $dv$ launched on a sufficiently small fixed patch at this entry escape towards the past to the same original end. For each fixed $\beta>0$, after fixing a sufficiently large radius and taking $T$ large, their bridge arrival points satisfy $$\label{bg:arrival-shell} (1-\beta)t_T<\|z-z_*\|<(1+\beta)t_T, \qquad z_*=C_e(t_T).$$ The entry crossing is unique and transverse, and separated from other used ray portions outside a uniform collar. Every fixed Hamiltonian flow jet, including launch-center derivatives and the propagators on all subsegments, is $e^{o(T)}$. Tensor parallel transport and its inverse are bounded. These assertions hold on short past and future launch enlargements through the deeper exit.
  - Explanation: Prepared launch covectors escape to the original end, with transverse entry crossing, controlled tensor transport and subexponential Hamiltonian flow jets on every used subsegment.
- **claim-normalized-wave-coefficients** (bound, [source](#stmt-bg-coefficients)): Let $V_\alpha$ be the matrix of $\nabla_{Z_\alpha}$ on the normalized vector basis. These matrices are bounded, and in the bulk limit $\min(v,-u,t)\to\infty$, $$\label{bg:connection-limits} V_0\longrightarrow D_0=\operatorname{diag}(-\kappa/2,\kappa/2,0,0), \qquad V_1\longrightarrow-D_0,\qquad V_A\longrightarrow0.$$ The differentiated connection terms contracted in the tensor operator $a\square_g$, and every normalized entry of $a\operatorname{Riem}(g)$, are bounded and tend to zero in this limit. Their fixed ordinary jets and those of the brackets of $Z$ are $e^{o(T)}$ on Equation <a href="#bg:truncation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bg:truncation">[bg:truncation]</a>. Moreover $\nabla_{L_1}L_1=\ell_1L_1$ exactly. The undifferentiated constant connection products are retained: on entry $n$ the limiting radial operator is $$\label{bg:radial-conjugation} \begin{split} &-(L_0+c_n)(L_1-c_n)-(L_1-c_n)(L_0+c_n)\\ &\qquad=-L_0L_1-L_1L_0+2c_n(L_0-L_1)+2c_n^2. \end{split}$$ It is the radial part of $e^{2c_nx}a\square_g^{\rm scal}e^{-2c_nx}$.
  - Explanation: Normalized coefficient arrays separate degenerating lapse factors from bounded geometric coefficients. Constant radial tensor connection products remain in the limiting operator rather than being discarded as small errors.
- **claim-linear-interior-packet-exterior-beam** (construction, [source](#stmt-bg-packet)): Fix any finite list of output derivative orders, including an exterior base buffer $N_b$. For every fixed $\zeta>0$ and every subsequently prescribed inverse accuracy $M_1$, sufficiently high finite transport and Taylor orders yield a real interior tensor $h_T^p$ and a real exterior tensor $h_T^B$ with the following properties. Put $$\label{bg:packet-scales} A_T=e^{-\alpha T},\qquad \alpha=7\kappa/8, \qquad\lambda_T=e^{\zeta T}.$$ The interior field has the form $$\label{bg:packet-form} h_T^p=A_T\operatorname{tr.rev.}_g\operatorname{Re} \left(e^{i\lambda_Tv}\sum_{j=0}^{J}\lambda_T^{-j}p_j\right).$$ Every fixed normalized coefficient jet of $p_j$ is $e^{o(T)}$. The field is supported in a fixed-width neighborhood of $v=T$, with angular support in the transported launch patch. Its relative array $q_T=(h_T^p(\hat e_\alpha,\hat e_\beta))$ satisfies $$\label{bg:packet-size} \\|q_T\\|_{C^N(\mathcal D_T)} \le A_T\lambda_T^N e^{o(T)}$$ at each specified order. The tensor $p_0$ is trace-free and $p_0(L_0,\cdot)=0$. There is a purely angular $\gamma$-unit vector $X$ at $p_T$ for which $$\label{bg:polarization} \sigma_T:=\left\|\operatorname{Re} \bigl(e^{i\lambda_TT}p_0(X,X)\bigr)\right\|$$ has two-sided subexponential size. The $a$-scaled linear wave residual and $\sqrt a$-scaled linear gauge residual, in normalized slots and with all prescribed ordinary derivatives, are bounded by $A_T\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$.* *In regular exterior lab components, $$\label{bg:beam-estimates} \\|h_T^B\\|_{C^{N_b}}\le A_T\lambda_T^{N_b+3/2}e^{o(T)}.$$ Its linear wave and gauge residuals, and its difference from $h_T^p$ on the entry collar, have size $A_T\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$ at all the prescribed finite orders. The latter assertion includes normal spacetime jets. On the original bridge its support lies in Equation <a href="#bg:arrival-shell" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bg:arrival-shell">[bg:arrival-shell]</a> with arbitrarily thin tube margins. Increasing $M_1$ and the finite formal degrees does not increase the positive frequency powers in Equations <a href="#bg:packet-size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bg:packet-size">[bg:packet-size]</a> and <a href="#bg:beam-estimates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bg:beam-estimates">[bg:beam-estimates]</a>. Thresholds and subexponential constants may depend on those degrees.
  - Explanation: Constrained interior transport and complex exterior beam superposition produce matching real fields with controlled finite jets, residuals and bridge support. Raising inverse accuracy does not raise the positive frequency powers at fixed output orders.
- **claim-linear-amplitude-independence** (construction, [source](#stmt-bg-amplitude-independent)): Fix any $\alpha>0$ and put $A_T=e^{-\alpha T}$. With this substitution, all linear conclusions of Proposition <a href="#bg:packet" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bg:packet">21</a> hold, with the same order of finite output, frequency and inverse-accuracy choices. The branch observer needs no bounded-curvature hypothesis.
  - Explanation: The linear construction is homogeneous in amplitude, so changing its exponential decay rate preserves the linear packet, beam and matching conclusions after the stated finite choices.
- **claim-signed-vacuum-data-entry** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-signed-entry)): Fix a finite data order $m\ge10$, a finite entry order $r$, a loss $\eta>0$, and $0<\nu_0<1$. After fixing all the finite derivative orders needed below, one can choose $\zeta>0$ and then the finite formal accuracy orders in Proposition <a href="#bg:packet" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bg:packet">21</a> so that the following hold for all sufficiently large parameters $T$ in the packet sequence.* *There are smooth positive complete vacuum data $d_T^\pm\in\mathcal D$ on the whole bridge, with $$\label{prep:data-convergence} p_m(d_T^\pm-d)\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular $d_T^\pm\in\mathcal U$ for large $T$. They agree exactly with $d$ on the other end, the connecting region, and a protected region containing every fixed compact subset for large $T$. On the selected asymptotic end the protected ball is $$\label{prep:protected-ball} B_T=\{\|z-z_*\|<\rho_T\},\qquad z_*=C_e(t_T),\qquad \rho_T=(1-2\beta)t_T,$$ where $\beta>0$ is fixed sufficiently small. The data have the weight-one metric and weight-two second-form symbol bounds at every order. Their full correction tails are retained; asymptotic coefficients are allowed to change.* *Ordinary exterior vacuum evolutions $g_T^\pm$, in exact wave-map gauge with the same target $g$, exist on the finite domains needed to reach $\mathcal S_T$, with fixed overlap margins and strict future-outflow artificial boundaries. Set $$H_T^\pm=\widehat{g_T^\pm-g},\qquad F_T^\pm=H_T^\pm\mp q_T \quad\hbox{on the entry collar}.$$ Then $$\label{prep:entry-estimates} \mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^\pm)\le A_T^2e^{\eta T},\qquad \mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^+-F_T^-)\le A_T^3e^{\eta T}.$$ The exact changes, packet, and errors vanish on the earlier needed entry interval $v<(1-\nu_0)T$. In particular, $r=61$ is permitted, with an arbitrarily small prescribed loss. The scaled linear deep residual can simultaneously be at most $e^{-4\kappa T}$ through order $60$, or at any other finite orders fixed in advance. The same statement holds for the separately prepared exchanged branch.
  - Explanation: Opposite beam seeds, a common constraint inverse and a common exterior gauge yield complete exact vacuum data close in a prescribed finite seminorm. Their entry errors are quadratic individually and cubic after subtraction, with exact early agreement.
- **claim-common-inverse-signed-correction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-constraint-pair)): Let $s\ge3$. Let $D_\pm=(\delta+a_\pm,b_\pm)$ be smooth positive data on $\mathbb R^3$ with symbol orders $-1,-2$ at every order, and suppose $$\\|D_\pm-(\delta,0)\\|_{X^s}\le\varepsilon_s.$$ Suppose the smooth compact sources $\phi_\pm$ are supported in $\{\|y'\|\ge1\}$ and equal $\Phi(D_\pm)$ there. For sufficiently small $\varepsilon_s$ and $b_2:=\max_\pm\\|\phi_\pm\\|_{Y^s}$ there are corrections $v_\pm$ supported in $\{\|y'\|\ge1\}$ such that $$\Phi(D_\pm+v_\pm)=0\quad (\|y'\|\ge1),\qquad \\|v_\pm\\|_{X^s}\le C_s b_2.$$ They are obtained with the same $\mathcal K$ and satisfy $$\label{prep:abstract-odd-correction} \\|v_+-v_-\\|_{X^s} \le C_s\left(\\|\phi_+-\phi_-\\|_{Y^s} +\\|D_+-D_-\\|_{X^s}b_2\right).$$ Each correction is smooth across the unit sphere and has symbol bounds of orders $-1,-2$ at every derivative order. Only the stated base norms have to be small. No vacuum equation is required for $D_\pm$ in the artificial interior $\|y'\|<1$.
  - Explanation: A shared support-preserving fixed-point solve corrects both exterior constraint defects and controls their difference. Smoothness at every order follows for the same corrections without requiring high-order smallness.
- **claim-general-amplitude-exterior-preparation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prep-amplitude-independent-exterior)): Fix any $\alpha>0$. Replace the amplitude in this section by $A_T=e^{-\alpha T}$ and use Corollary <a href="#bg:amplitude-independent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bg:amplitude-independent">23</a>. For every fixed data order $m$, entry order $r$, loss $\eta>0$ and late-support fraction $0<\nu_0<1$, the conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prep:signed-entry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:signed-entry">24</a> hold after choosing the frequency exponent sufficiently small and the inverse accuracy sufficiently large. This assertion ends at the entry cylinder.
  - Explanation: Signed preparation extends to any fixed positive amplitude decay rate after reducing frequency losses and raising inverse accuracy. This corollary stops at the entry cylinder.
- **claim-signed-interior-evolution** (construction, [source](#stmt-energy-signed-experiment)): Fix $0<\delta<\kappa/32$ and the prescribed finite data seminorm. Use Proposition <a href="#bg:packet" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bg:packet">21</a> and Proposition <a href="#prep:signed-entry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:signed-entry">24</a> with sufficiently high fixed orders, sufficiently small losses and late support parameter, and sufficiently accurate linear residuals. For sufficiently small fixed $\zeta>0$ and sufficiently large $T$, the two entry data have smooth vacuum evolutions $g_T^\pm$ on the strict domain <a href="#energy:domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="energy:domain">[energy:domain]</a>, in wave-map gauge with target $g$. Let $H_T^\pm$ be the relative arrays of $g_T^\pm-g$, and put $$F_T^\pm=H_T^\pm\mp q_T,\qquad F_T^{\mathrm{odd}}=F_T^+-F_T^-.$$ Then $$\label{energy:conclusion} \begin{aligned} \lVert F_T^\pm\rVert_{C^{30}_{\mathrm{mix}}(\Omega_T)} &\le e^{(\kappa-2\alpha+\delta)T},\\ \lVert F_T^{\mathrm{odd}}\rVert_{C^2_{\mathrm{mix}}(\Omega_T)} &\le e^{(\kappa-3\alpha+\delta)T} +e^{(2\kappa-4\alpha+\delta)T}. \end{aligned}$$ The exact changes and all three error fields have support in $v\ge(1-\nu_0)T-o(1)$. The time slices remain spacelike and the two moving side faces remain strict future exits. Moreover, for some $c>0$, $$\label{energy:small-relative} \lVert H_T^\pm\rVert_{C^{30}_{\mathrm{mix}}} \le e^{-(\kappa/2+c)T},\qquad e^{o(T)}\left(\lVert F_T^{\mathrm{odd}}\rVert_{C^2_{\mathrm{mix}}} +\sum_\pm\lVert H_T^\pm\rVert_{C^2_{\mathrm{mix}}}^2\right) =o(A_T).$$ Here every subexponential factor is at a fixed finite order. No higher seminorm of the fixed smooth background is required to be small.
  - Explanation: The exact signed interior evolutions retain strict outflow boundaries and satisfy individual and odd error bounds. Odd cancellation and quadratic relative smallness suffice for curvature separation even when an individual error exceeds the packet.
- **claim-finite-volterra-propagator** (bound, [source](#stmt-energy-volterra)): For levels $0,\ldots,K$ satisfying <a href="#energy:hierarchy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="energy:hierarchy">[energy:hierarchy]</a>, with fixed $K$, there is a subexponential $P_T$, uniform for all $0\le s\le t\le L$, such that $$\label{energy:propagator} \sum_{j\le K}E_j(t) \le P_T\left\{ e^{\mathcal R_T(t)-\mathcal R_T(s)}\sum_{j\le K}E_j(s) +\int_s^t e^{\mathcal R_T(t)-\mathcal R_T(\tau)} \sum_{j\le K}S_j(\tau)\,d\tau\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: Strictly lower triangular couplings give a finite time-ordered propagator. Its subexponential factor is uniform in both interval endpoints and preserves the common intermediate-time growth difference.
- **claim-whole-bridge-attachment** (construction, [source](#stmt-attach-development)): For all sufficiently large finite $T$, there is a smooth time-oriented vacuum development $\mathcal N_T^\pm$ of the entire datum $d_T^\pm$ with $\Sigma$ as a Cauchy hypersurface. It contains the constructed finite observation, restricted within its construction margins, and an optical-coordinate box about $p_T$, of fixed sufficiently small coordinate radii independent of $T$, in $I^+_{\mathcal N_T^\pm}(\Sigma)$. It admits a time-orientation-preserving open isometric embedding into $\mathcal M_{d_T^\pm}$ that preserves the initial-data embedding. The full physical constraint-correction tail is part of its initial data.
  - Explanation: Exact collar identifications and past-preserved strict domain gaps attach the finite experiment to a vacuum development of the entire corrected datum. Cauchyness is established before invoking the maximal-development embedding.
- **claim-fixed-canonical-state-tube** (bound, [source](#stmt-attach-canonical-tube)): There are a fixed late $v_0$ and a fixed bounded open canonical state set $\mathcal K$ such that the late track stays a fixed positive distance from $\partial\mathcal K$, its positions lie in one angular coordinate chart, and $p(v,Y)\ge p_*>0$ for every $v\ge v_0$ and $Y\in\mathcal K$. On a slightly enlarged such set, the background canonical vector field $V_0$ has $$\label{attach:integrable-rate} \|V_0(v,Y)\|+\|D_YV_0(v,Y)\| \le C e^{-\kappa v}e^{o(v)}=:\Lambda(v), \qquad \int_{v_0}^{\infty}\Lambda(v)\,dv<\infty.$$ The expression $e^{o(v)}$ here uses the current endpoint $v$, not the terminal experiment parameter $T$.
  - Explanation: The late observer lies in a fixed bounded state tube with positive shifted momentum and integrable background dynamics. Its derivative bound uses the current optical endpoint rather than the terminal experiment parameter.
- **claim-absolute-future-shell-root** (construction, [source](#stmt-attach-absolute-shell)): On the fixed canonical tube of Lemma <a href="#attach:canonical-tube" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="attach:canonical-tube">31</a>, intersected with the actual deep domain, the new unit shell has a simple root $z=z_T^\pm(v,Y)$ close to $z_0(v,Y)$, with $$\label{attach:root-bound} \lVert z_T^\pm-z_0\rVert_{C^2_Y} \le \varepsilon_T^{\mathrm{sh}},\qquad \varepsilon_T^{\mathrm{sh}}=\varepsilon_T e^{o(T)}.$$ The associated canonical vector field satisfies $\|V_T^\pm-V_0\|\le C\varepsilon_T^{\mathrm{sh}}$. On this root $dv/d\tau>0$. The estimates concern $\pi_v$ itself.
  - Explanation: Multiplying the perturbed unit shell by the lapse exposes a simple future root with absolute canonical momentum control. The estimate does not require the root error to be small relative to the lapse.
- **claim-adaptive-observer-test-frame** (construction, [source](#stmt-attach-observer)): At $p_T$ put $U_T^\pm=I_T^\pm U$. There are seeds $w_T^\pm\to w$ on $\Sigma$ whose future unit geodesics in $\mathcal M_{d_T^\pm}$ reach $p_T$ with tangent $U_T^\pm$, at finite positive proper times strictly before their full-MGHD future durations. In particular $w_T^\pm$ belong to the original open seed set for all sufficiently large $T$.* *Choose an angular orthonormal frame $e_A$ near $p_T$, including the packet polarization direction at the center, and set $$\label{attach:adapted-frame} E_0^\pm=I_T^\pm L_0,\qquad E_1^\pm=a^{-1}I_T^\pm L_1,\qquad E_A^\pm=I_T^\pm e_A.$$ At $p_T$ the Euclidean norm of components in this frame and the parallel positive test norm $k_{d_T^\pm,w_T^\pm}$ are uniformly equivalent, with constants independent of $T$. Their induced operator norms are uniformly equivalent as well.
  - Explanation: Prescribing the terminal tangent through the metric isometry and integrating backwards yields nearby initial seeds. The observer-adapted frame is uniformly comparable to the transported positive test norm.
- **claim-smooth-scaled-curvature-functional** (bound, [source](#stmt-holo-curvature-functional)): At $p_T$ define $$\label{holo:scaled-curvature} C_T^\pm=a_p^2\operatorname{Riem}(g_T^\pm) (E_1^\pm,E_2^\pm,E_1^\pm,E_2^\pm).$$ There is a smooth function $\mathcal C_T$ of the relative metric array and its ordinary jets through order two such that $C_T^\pm=\mathcal C_T(H_T^\pm)$. On a fixed small neighborhood of the zero relative array its first two derivatives have norm at most $e^{o(T)}$. Consequently $$\label{holo:signed-taylor} C_T^+-C_T^-=2D\mathcal C_T(0)[q_T]+o(A_T).$$
  - Explanation: Scaled curvature is a smooth function of a finite relative metric jet with subexponential derivative bounds. Subtraction cancels the background term and leaves the linear signed signal plus controlled remainders.
- **claim-large-signed-curvature-signal** (bound, [source](#stmt-holo-signal)): For every sufficiently large $T$, at least one sign satisfies $\|C_T^\pm\|>A_T$.
  - Explanation: The leading phase derivatives and nonvanishing polarization separate the signed curvature observations enough that at least one exceeds the packet amplitude.
- **claim-boosted-connection-bracket-jets** (bound, [source](#stmt-holo-boosted-control)): The coordinate components of $f_\alpha$, the arrays $G^\mu{}_{\alpha\beta}$, and the coefficients $B^\mu{}_{\alpha\beta}$ of $[f_\alpha,f_\beta]$ in the $f$ frame have ordinary jets through order twenty bounded by $e^{o(T)}$.
  - Explanation: Null metric compatibility eliminates the worst boosted connection entries. Remaining potentially growing entries have vanishing background values and sufficiently small perturbations, controlling connection and bracket jets.
- **claim-short-flow-loops-holonomy-jets** (construction, [source](#stmt-holo-loops)): For sufficiently large $T$, every loop in Equation <a href="#holo:surface" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="holo:surface">[holo:surface]</a> is defined inside the attached future observation box. There is $Q_T=e^{o(T)}$ such that $$\label{holo:loop-estimates} \mathcal L_T(h)\leq a_phQ_T,\qquad \sup_{0\leq h\leq17h_T}\\|\mathcal H_T^{(18)}(h)\\|\leq Q_T.$$ Moreover, $$\label{holo:second-coefficient} \mathcal H_T(0)=\operatorname{Id},\qquad \frac12\mathcal H_T''(0)=\pm a_p^2R^{g'}(E_1,E_2)(p_T)$$ as endomorphism matrices in the based frame.
  - Explanation: Actual parameter-dependent flows generate loops inside the attached observation box. Frame variation preserves the lapse factor in developed length, while transport differentiation controls the finite holonomy jet and identifies its quadratic curvature coefficient.
- **claim-quadratic-interpolation-extraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-holo-interpolation)): Let $F:[0,17h]\to V$ be $C^{18}$, where $V$ is a finite-dimensional normed vector space and $h>0$. Put $$S_1=\sum_{r=1}^{17}\frac1r,\qquad S_2=\sum_{r=1}^{17}\frac1{r^2},\qquad c_0=\frac{S_1^2-S_2}{2},\qquad c_j=\frac{(-1)^j}{j}\binom{17}{j} \left(S_1-\frac1j\right)\quad(1\leq j\leq17).$$ Then the following identity holds with Bochner integrals in $V$: $$\label{holo:exact-interpolation} \frac{F''(0)}2 =h^{-2}\sum_{j=0}^{17}c_jF(jh) -\frac{h^{-2}}{17!}\sum_{j=0}^{17}c_j \int_0^{jh}(jh-t)^{17}F^{(18)}(t)\,dt.$$ In particular, if $F(0)=0$, $\\|F(jh)\\|\leq b jh$, and $\sup\\|F^{(18)}\\|\leq M$, then $$\label{holo:interpolation-bound} \left\\|\frac{F''(0)}2\right\\| \leq K_1\frac b h+K_2 M h^{16},\qquad K_1=\sum_{j=1}^{17}\|c_j\|j,\quad K_2=\frac1{18!}\sum_{j=1}^{17}\|c_j\|j^{18}.$$ Both constants are finite and independent of $F,h,T$.
  - Explanation: Explicit Lagrange coefficient weights extract the quadratic Taylor coefficient from sampled values. An integral remainder bounds extraction error using the stated higher derivative norm.
- **claim-finite-transport-test-violation** (construction, [source](#stmt-holo-violation)): Fix a passing test $\mathcal A(O,B)$, a datum $d\in\mathcal A(O,B)$, and a branch seed $w\in O$ as in Corollary <a href="#test:branch-seed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:branch-seed">15</a>. For every finite $m\geq10$ the signed experiments can be chosen with $p_m(d_T^\pm-d)\to0$ such that, for every sufficiently large $T$, at least one of $d_T^+$ and $d_T^-$ does not belong to $\mathcal A(O,B)$.
  - Explanation: At least one arbitrarily nearby signed datum violates the passing test. Assuming it still passes forces the extracted scaled curvature below the signed signal, giving a finite loop contradiction in the full development.
- **claim-nearby-trivial-spatial-data-isometries** (construction, [source](#stmt-phase-trivial-isometries)): Every neighborhood of $d_*$ in $\mathcal D$ contains complete vacuum data whose spatial data isometry group is trivial.
  - Explanation: Compact normal graphs in Kerr produce exact nearby vacuum data whose trace identifies a unique point and fixes every tangent direction, forcing any spatial data isometry to be the identity.

        "anchor": "stmt-test-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "After decreasing the defining $p_{10}$ radius of $\\mathcal U$, each $d\\in\\mathcal U$ has a smooth globally hyperbolic vacuum comparison development $\\mathcal P_d$ containing the whole bridge $\\Sigma$ as a Cauchy hypersurface and embedded openly and isometrically in $\\mathcal M_d$. It contains a spacelike cylinder $\\mathcal S$ and its full future product wedge, with coordinates $t=(u+v)/2\\ge0$, $x=(v-u)/2\\in\\mathbb R$, $\\theta\\in\\mathbb S^2$, in which $$\\label{test:wedge}\n \\begin{split}\n g&=-2a\\,du\\,dv+\n \\gamma_{AB}(d\\theta^A-b^Adu)(d\\theta^B-b^Bdu),\\\\\n a&=q\\mathcal A,\\qquad\n q=q_0e^{-\\kappa(u+v)},\\qquad q_0,\\kappa>0.\n \\end{split}$$ The coefficients $\\mathcal A^{\\pm1}$ and $\\gamma^{\\pm1}$ are uniformly bounded, with $\\gamma$ uniformly comparable to a fixed round sphere metric.       ],
      "exact_statement": "After decreasing the defining $p_{10}$ radius of $\\mathcal U$, each $d\\in\\mathcal U$ has a smooth globally hyperbolic vacuum comparison development $\\mathcal P_d$ containing the whole bridge $\\Sigma$ as a Cauchy hypersurface and embedded openly and isometrically in $\\mathcal M_d$. It contains a spacelike cylinder $\\mathcal S$ and its full future product wedge, with coordinates $t=(u+v)/2\\ge0$, $x=(v-u)/2\\in\\mathbb R$, $\\theta\\in\\mathbb S^2$, in which $$\\label{test:wedge}\n \\begin{split}\n g&=-2a\\,du\\,dv+\n \\gamma_{AB}(d\\theta^A-b^Adu)(d\\theta^B-b^Bdu),\\\\\n a&=q\\mathcal A,\\qquad\n q=q_0e^{-\\kappa(u+v)},\\qquad q_0,\\kappa>0.\n \\end{split}$$ The coefficients $\\mathcal A^{\\pm1}$ and $\\gamma^{\\pm1}$ are uniformly bounded, with $\\gamma$ uniformly comparable to a fixed round sphere metric. Every seed with $T_d(w)<\\infty$ crosses $\\mathcal S$ before its first runout from $\\mathcal P_d$, and that first runout has exactly one of the following coordinate limits: $$\\label{test:runout}\n \\begin{array}{lll}\n \\text{first branch:}&u\\to u_*<\\infty,&v\\to+\\infty,\\\\\n \\text{second branch:}&v\\to v_*<\\infty,&u\\to+\\infty,\\\\\n \\text{corner:}&u\\to+\\infty,&v\\to+\\infty.\n \\end{array}$$ These alternatives describe limiting coordinate regimes; they do not adjoin boundary points or identify $\\mathcal P_d$ with $\\mathcal M_d$.",
      "explanation": "The comparison development contains the complete initial bridge and a full optical wedge. Finite-duration seeds first run out through a branch or the corner, as coordinate limits rather than adjoined boundary points.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bg-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "There is a choice of $\\epsilon>0$, depending only on the fixed Kerr center and the fixed preparation, such that every $p_{10}(d-d_*)<\\epsilon$ in the stated smooth symbol class has the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The cylinder $\\mathcal S$ in Proposition <a href=\"#test:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"test:comparison\">12</a> has a smooth vacuum development on the full product wedge $[0,\\infty)_t\\times\\mathbb R_x\\times\\mathbb S^2$ in Equation <a href=\"#bg:wedge\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:wedge\">[bg:wedge]</a>. The quantities $\\mathcal A^{\\pm1},\\gamma^{\\pm1}$ are uniformly bounded in angular $H^6$, and $\\xi_i,a^{-1}\\partial_vb$ in angular $H^5$. There are nonnegative functions $g_i(y_i)$ with summable unit-cell suprema such that, in the indicated low angular norms, $$\\label{bg:envelopes}\n     \\|\\chi_i,\\ell_i+\\kappa\\|_{H^4}\\le C(q+g_i(y_i)),\n     \\qquad \\|b\\|_{H^5}\\le C(q+g_0(u)).$$*\n\n\n2.  *The following quantities are uniformly bounded, and tend to zero when $t,|u|,|v|\\to\\infty$: $$\\label{bg:low-limits}\n    \\begin{gathered}\n     \\|b\\|_{H^5}+\\sum_i\\|\\chi_i,\\ell_i+\\kappa\\|_{H^4},\\\\\n     \\sum_{k=0}^1\\left(\\|\\partial_{y_k}b\\|_{H^2}\n           +\\sum_i\\|\\partial_{y_k}\\chi_i,\n                              \\partial_{y_k}\\ell_i\\|_{H^2}\\right),\\\\\n     \\sum_{k=0}^1\\left(\\|\\partial_{y_k}\\gamma\\|_{H^4}\n                     +\\|\\partial_{y_k}\\log a+\\kappa\\|_{H^4}\\right).\n    \\end{gathered}$$ In particular $D\\log a,D\\gamma,b,Db$ are uniformly bounded.*\n\n\n\n3.  *For each fixed $U\\in\\mathbb R$, $C>0$, and finite order, all ordinary derivatives of $$\\label{bg:high-arrays}\n     \\gamma^{\\pm1},\\quad\\log\\mathcal A,\\quad b,\n                      \\quad a^{-1}\\partial_vb$$ are bounded by $e^{o(T)}$ on $$\\label{bg:truncation}\n     t\\ge0,\\qquad y_d\\le U,\\qquad y_l\\le CT,\n     \\qquad \\{d,l\\}=\\{0,1\\}.$$ This includes every restriction and coordinate-path subsegment. Every fixed derivative of $a$, $\\sqrt a$, or $\\partial_vb$ retains, respectively, the factor $a$, $\\sqrt a$, or $a$.*\n\n4.  *Either branch can be put in the convention $v\\to\\infty$, $u\\to u_*<\\infty$. Exchanging the optical labels and preparing the angular coordinates anew preserves all these conclusions on the same product spacetime and initial cylinder.*\n\n*Only $p_{10}$ is required small; no higher seminorm, radiation coefficient, or curvature measured by a timelike observer is required small or bounded.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bg-branch",
        "exact_quote": "Let a future unit timelike geodesic approach a branch $v\\to\\infty$, $u\\to u_*<\\infty$, in the comparison wedge. With proper time denoted by $\\tau$, put $$p=a\\dot v,\\qquad \\Xi=\\dot\\theta-b\\dot u,\\qquad P=\\gamma\\Xi.$$ Then $p\\asymp1$, and $P$, $\\Xi$, and $\\dot u$ stay bounded on a late segment. The angular position has a limit. At the point $p_T$ with $v=T$ one has $$\\label{bg:branch-scales}\n             a_p:=a(p_T)\\asymp e^{-\\kappa T},\n             \\qquad \\dot v(p_T)\\asymp e^{\\kappa T}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-264.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bg-exterior",
        "exact_quote": "On either prepared end use regular exterior time $t_e$, co-moving coordinates $y$, and lab coordinates $z=y+C_e(t_e)$, with fixed $\\sup|C_e'|\\le s_*<1/10$, after decreasing $\\epsilon$ if needed. The relevant slice $t_e=0$ agrees with the original bridge sufficiently far out. There is a preparation parameter $I$ with $0<c_I\\le I'\\le C_I$ and $I(t_T)=T$. The launch patch near $v=x=T$ on $\\mathcal S$ occurs at $t_e=t_T+O(1)$, and $t_T\\asymp T$. A fixed enlargement is contained in $t_e\\le t_T+C_2$. Its selected-end portion $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$ is earlier by at least $c\\nu_0T-O(1)$ for each fixed $\\nu_0>0$.*\n\n*The exterior metric has uniformly regular spacelike temporal levels. Its first and second ordinary lab derivatives are bounded on the computing region and become arbitrarily small at sufficiently large co-radius, uniformly in time. Every fixed mixed jet on the used time and radius ranges $O(1+T)$ is $e^{o(T)}$. Entry charts have a noncharacteristic collar with controlled low jets and subexponential fixed higher jets; the inner exit has a strict margin deeper than the entry cylinder.*\n\n\n*The null covectors $dv$ launched on a sufficiently small fixed patch at this entry escape towards the past to the same original end. For each fixed $\\beta>0$, after fixing a sufficiently large radius and taking $T$ large, their bridge arrival points satisfy $$\\label{bg:arrival-shell}\n (1-\\beta)t_T<|z-z_*|<(1+\\beta)t_T,\n                  \\qquad z_*=C_e(t_T).$$ The entry crossing is unique and transverse, and separated from other used ray portions outside a uniform collar. Every fixed Hamiltonian flow jet, including launch-center derivatives and the propagators on all subsegments, is $e^{o(T)}$. Tensor parallel transport and its inverse are bounded. These assertions hold on short past and future launch enlargements through the deeper exit.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.2"
      },

      ],
      "exact_statement": "On either prepared end use regular exterior time $t_e$, co-moving coordinates $y$, and lab coordinates $z=y+C_e(t_e)$, with fixed $\\sup|C_e'|\\le s_*<1/10$, after decreasing $\\epsilon$ if needed. The relevant slice $t_e=0$ agrees with the original bridge sufficiently far out. There is a preparation parameter $I$ with $0<c_I\\le I'\\le C_I$ and $I(t_T)=T$. The launch patch near $v=x=T$ on $\\mathcal S$ occurs at $t_e=t_T+O(1)$, and $t_T\\asymp T$. A fixed enlargement is contained in $t_e\\le t_T+C_2$. Its selected-end portion $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$ is earlier by at least $c\\nu_0T-O(1)$ for each fixed $\\nu_0>0$.*\n\n*The exterior metric has uniformly regular spacelike temporal levels. Its first and second ordinary lab derivatives are bounded on the computing region and become arbitrarily small at sufficiently large co-radius, uniformly in time. Every fixed mixed jet on the used time and radius ranges $O(1+T)$ is $e^{o(T)}$. Entry charts have a noncharacteristic collar with controlled low jets and subexponential fixed higher jets; the inner exit has a strict margin deeper than the entry cylinder.*\n\n\n*The null covectors $dv$ launched on a sufficiently small fixed patch at this entry escape towards the past to the same original end. For each fixed $\\beta>0$, after fixing a sufficiently large radius and taking $T$ large, their bridge arrival points satisfy $$\\label{bg:arrival-shell}\n (1-\\beta)t_T<|z-z_*|<(1+\\beta)t_T,\n                  \\qquad z_*=C_e(t_T).$$ The entry crossing is unique and transverse, and separated from other used ray portions outside a uniform collar. Every fixed Hamiltonian flow jet, including launch-center derivatives and the propagators on all subsegments, is $e^{o(T)}$. Tensor parallel transport and its inverse are bounded. These assertions hold on short past and future launch enlargements through the deeper exit.",
      "explanation": "Prepared launch covectors escape to the original end, with transverse entry crossing, controlled tensor transport and subexponential Hamiltonian flow jets on every used subsegment.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bg-coefficients",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V_\\alpha$ be the matrix of $\\nabla_{Z_\\alpha}$ on the normalized vector basis. These matrices are bounded, and in the bulk limit $\\min(v,-u,t)\\to\\infty$, $$\\label{bg:connection-limits}\n V_0\\longrightarrow D_0=\\operatorname{diag}(-\\kappa/2,\\kappa/2,0,0),\n \\qquad V_1\\longrightarrow-D_0,\\qquad V_A\\longrightarrow0.$$ The differentiated connection terms contracted in the tensor operator $a\\square_g$, and every normalized entry of $a\\operatorname{Riem}(g)$, are bounded and tend to zero in this limit. Their fixed ordinary jets and those of the brackets of $Z$ are $e^{o(T)}$ on Equation <a href=\"#bg:truncation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:truncation\">[bg:truncation]</a>. Moreover $\\nabla_{L_1}L_1=\\ell_1L_1$ exactly. The undifferentiated constant connection products are retained: on entry $n$ the limiting radial operator is $$\\label{bg:radial-conjugation}\n\\begin{split}\n &-(L_0+c_n)(L_1-c_n)-(L_1-c_n)(L_0+c_n)\\\\\n &\\qquad=-L_0L_1-L_1L_0+2c_n(L_0-L_1)+2c_n^2.\n\\end{split}$$ It is the radial part of $e^{2c_nx}a\\square_g^{\\rm scal}e^{-2c_nx}$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bg-packet",
        "exact_quote": "Fix any finite list of output derivative orders, including an exterior base buffer $N_b$. For every fixed $\\zeta>0$ and every subsequently prescribed inverse accuracy $M_1$, sufficiently high finite transport and Taylor orders yield a real interior tensor $h_T^p$ and a real exterior tensor $h_T^B$ with the following properties. Put $$\\label{bg:packet-scales}\n       A_T=e^{-\\alpha T},\\qquad \\alpha=7\\kappa/8,\n       \\qquad\\lambda_T=e^{\\zeta T}.$$ The interior field has the form $$\\label{bg:packet-form}\n h_T^p=A_T\\operatorname{tr.rev.}_g\\operatorname{Re}\n       \\left(e^{i\\lambda_Tv}\\sum_{j=0}^{J}\\lambda_T^{-j}p_j\\right).$$ Every fixed normalized coefficient jet of $p_j$ is $e^{o(T)}$. The field is supported in a fixed-width neighborhood of $v=T$, with angular support in the transported launch patch. Its relative array $q_T=(h_T^p(\\hat e_\\alpha,\\hat e_\\beta))$ satisfies $$\\label{bg:packet-size}\n              \\|q_T\\|_{C^N(\\mathcal D_T)}\n                    \\le A_T\\lambda_T^N e^{o(T)}$$ at each specified order. The tensor $p_0$ is trace-free and $p_0(L_0,\\cdot)=0$. There is a purely angular $\\gamma$-unit vector $X$ at $p_T$ for which $$\\label{bg:polarization}\n \\sigma_T:=\\left|\\operatorname{Re}\n        \\bigl(e^{i\\lambda_TT}p_0(X,X)\\bigr)\\right|$$ has two-sided subexponential size. The $a$-scaled linear wave residual and $\\sqrt a$-scaled linear gauge residual, in normalized slots and with all prescribed ordinary derivatives, are bounded by $A_T\\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$.*\n\n\n*In regular exterior lab components, $$\\label{bg:beam-estimates}\n \\|h_T^B\\|_{C^{N_b}}\\le A_T\\lambda_T^{N_b+3/2}e^{o(T)}.$$ Its linear wave and gauge residuals, and its difference from $h_T^p$ on the entry collar, have size $A_T\\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$ at all the prescribed finite orders. The latter assertion includes normal spacetime jets. On the original bridge its support lies in Equation <a href=\"#bg:arrival-shell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:arrival-shell\">[bg:arrival-shell]</a> with arbitrarily thin tube margins. Increasing $M_1$ and the finite formal degrees does not increase the positive frequency powers in Equations <a href=\"#bg:packet-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet-size\">[bg:packet-size]</a> and <a href=\"#bg:beam-estimates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:beam-estimates\">[bg:beam-estimates]</a>. Thresholds and subexponential constants may depend on those degrees.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-signed-entry",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite data order $m\\ge10$, a finite entry order $r$, a loss $\\eta>0$, and $0<\\nu_0<1$. After fixing all the finite derivative orders needed below, one can choose $\\zeta>0$ and then the finite formal accuracy orders in Proposition <a href=\"#bg:packet\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet\">21</a> so that the following hold for all sufficiently large parameters $T$ in the packet sequence.*\n\n\n*There are smooth positive complete vacuum data $d_T^\\pm\\in\\mathcal D$ on the whole bridge, with $$\\label{prep:data-convergence}\n p_m(d_T^\\pm-d)\\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular $d_T^\\pm\\in\\mathcal U$ for large $T$. They agree exactly with $d$ on the other end, the connecting region, and a protected region containing every fixed compact subset for large $T$. On the selected asymptotic end the protected ball is $$\\label{prep:protected-ball}\n B_T=\\{|z-z_*|<\\rho_T\\},\\qquad\n z_*=C_e(t_T),\\qquad \\rho_T=(1-2\\beta)t_T,$$ where $\\beta>0$ is fixed sufficiently small. The data have the weight-one metric and weight-two second-form symbol bounds at every order. Their full correction tails are retained; asymptotic coefficients are allowed to change.*\n\n\n*Ordinary exterior vacuum evolutions $g_T^\\pm$, in exact wave-map gauge with the same target $g$, exist on the finite domains needed to reach $\\mathcal S_T$, with fixed overlap margins and strict future-outflow artificial boundaries. Set $$H_T^\\pm=\\widehat{g_T^\\pm-g},\\qquad\n F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T\n \\quad\\hbox{on the entry collar}.$$ Then $$\\label{prep:entry-estimates}\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^\\pm)\\le A_T^2e^{\\eta T},\\qquad\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^+-F_T^-)\\le A_T^3e^{\\eta T}.$$ The exact changes, packet, and errors vanish on the earlier needed entry interval $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$. In particular, $r=61$ is permitted, with an arbitrarily small prescribed loss. The scaled linear deep residual can simultaneously be at most $e^{-4\\kappa T}$ through order $60$, or at any other finite orders fixed in advance. The same statement holds for the separately prepared exchanged branch.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

Set $$H_T^\\pm=\\widehat{g_T^\\pm-g},\\qquad\n F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T\n \\quad\\hbox{on the entry collar}.$$ Then $$\\label{prep:entry-estimates}\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^\\pm)\\le A_T^2e^{\\eta T},\\qquad\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^+-F_T^-)\\le A_T^3e^{\\eta T}.$$ The exact changes, packet, and errors vanish on the earlier needed entry interval $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$.       "exact_statement": "Fix a finite data order $m\\ge10$, a finite entry order $r$, a loss $\\eta>0$, and $0<\\nu_0<1$. The data have the weight-one metric and weight-two second-form symbol bounds at every order. Their full correction tails are retained; asymptotic coefficients are allowed to change.*\n\n\n*Ordinary exterior vacuum evolutions $g_T^\\pm$, in exact wave-map gauge with the same target $g$, exist on the finite domains needed to reach $\\mathcal S_T$, with fixed overlap margins and strict future-outflow artificial boundaries. Set $$H_T^\\pm=\\widehat{g_T^\\pm-g},\\qquad\n F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T\n \\quad\\hbox{on the entry collar}.$$ Then $$\\label{prep:entry-estimates}\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^\\pm)\\le A_T^2e^{\\eta T},\\qquad\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^+-F_T^-)\\le A_T^3e^{\\eta T}.$$ The exact changes, packet, and errors vanish on the earlier needed entry interval $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$. In particular, $r=61$ is permitted, with an arbitrarily small prescribed loss. The scaled linear deep residual can simultaneously be at most $e^{-4\\kappa T}$ through order $60$, or at any other finite orders fixed in advance. The same statement holds for the separately prepared exchanged branch.",
      "explanation": "Opposite beam seeds, a common constraint inverse and a common exterior gauge yield complete exact vacuum data close in a prescribed finite seminorm. Their entry errors are quadratic individually and cubic after subtraction, with exact early agreement.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $s\\ge3$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-energy-signed-experiment",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\delta<\\kappa/32$ and the prescribed finite data seminorm. Use Proposition <a href=\"#bg:packet\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet\">21</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prep:signed-entry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prep:signed-entry\">24</a> with sufficiently high fixed orders, sufficiently small losses and late support parameter, and sufficiently accurate linear residuals. For sufficiently small fixed $\\zeta>0$ and sufficiently large $T$, the two entry data have smooth vacuum evolutions $g_T^\\pm$ on the strict domain <a href=\"#energy:domain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"energy:domain\">[energy:domain]</a>, in wave-map gauge with target $g$. Let $H_T^\\pm$ be the relative arrays of $g_T^\\pm-g$, and put $$F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T,\\qquad\n F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}=F_T^+-F_T^-.$$ Then $$\\label{energy:conclusion}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert F_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^{30}_{\\mathrm{mix}}(\\Omega_T)}\n   &\\le e^{(\\kappa-2\\alpha+\\delta)T},\\\\\n \\lVert F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}(\\Omega_T)}\n   &\\le e^{(\\kappa-3\\alpha+\\delta)T}\n            +e^{(2\\kappa-4\\alpha+\\delta)T}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The exact changes and all three error fields have support in $v\\ge(1-\\nu_0)T-o(1)$. The time slices remain spacelike and the two moving side faces remain strict future exits. Moreover, for some $c>0$, $$\\label{energy:small-relative}\n \\lVert H_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^{30}_{\\mathrm{mix}}}\n       \\le e^{-(\\kappa/2+c)T},\\qquad\n e^{o(T)}\\left(\\lVert F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}}\n          +\\sum_\\pm\\lVert H_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}}^2\\right)\n       =o(A_T).$$ Here every subexponential factor is at a fixed finite order. No higher seminorm of the fixed smooth background is required to be small.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-energy-volterra",
        "exact_quote": "For levels $0,\\ldots,K$ satisfying <a href=\"#energy:hierarchy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"energy:hierarchy\">[energy:hierarchy]</a>, with fixed $K$, there is a subexponential $P_T$, uniform for all $0\\le s\\le t\\le L$, such that $$\\label{energy:propagator}\n \\sum_{j\\le K}E_j(t)\n \\le P_T\\left\\{\n e^{\\mathcal R_T(t)-\\mathcal R_T(s)}\\sum_{j\\le K}E_j(s)\n +\\int_s^t e^{\\mathcal R_T(t)-\\mathcal R_T(\\tau)}\n                       \\sum_{j\\le K}S_j(\\tau)\\,d\\tau\\right\\}.$$",

        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large finite $T$, there is a smooth time-oriented vacuum development $\\mathcal N_T^\\pm$ of the entire datum $d_T^\\pm$ with $\\Sigma$ as a Cauchy hypersurface.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-attach-canonical-tube",
        "exact_quote": "There are a fixed late $v_0$ and a fixed bounded open canonical state set $\\mathcal K$ such that the late track stays a fixed positive distance from $\\partial\\mathcal K$, its positions lie in one angular coordinate chart, and $p(v,Y)\\ge p_*>0$ for every $v\\ge v_0$ and $Y\\in\\mathcal K$.         "exact_quote": "At $p_T$ put $U_T^\\pm=I_T^\\pm U$. In particular $w_T^\\pm$ belong to the original open seed set for all sufficiently large $T$.*\n\n\n*Choose an angular orthonormal frame $e_A$ near $p_T$, including the packet polarization direction at the center, and set $$\\label{attach:adapted-frame}\n E_0^\\pm=I_T^\\pm L_0,\\qquad\n E_1^\\pm=a^{-1}I_T^\\pm L_1,\\qquad\n E_A^\\pm=I_T^\\pm e_A.$$ At $p_T$ the Euclidean norm of components in this frame and the parallel positive test norm $k_{d_T^\\pm,w_T^\\pm}$ are uniformly equivalent, with constants independent of $T$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-holo-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F:[0,17h]\\to V$ be $C^{18}$, where $V$ is a finite-dimensional normed vector space and $h>0$. Put $$S_1=\\sum_{r=1}^{17}\\frac1r,\\qquad\n S_2=\\sum_{r=1}^{17}\\frac1{r^2},\\qquad\n c_0=\\frac{S_1^2-S_2}{2},\\qquad\n c_j=\\frac{(-1)^j}{j}\\binom{17}{j}\n                   \\left(S_1-\\frac1j\\right)\\quad(1\\leq j\\leq17).$$ Then the following identity holds with Bochner integrals in $V$: $$\\label{holo:exact-interpolation}\n \\frac{F''(0)}2\n  =h^{-2}\\sum_{j=0}^{17}c_jF(jh)\n    -\\frac{h^{-2}}{17!}\\sum_{j=0}^{17}c_j\n       \\int_0^{jh}(jh-t)^{17}F^{(18)}(t)\\,dt.$$ In particular, if $F(0)=0$, $\\|F(jh)\\|\\leq b jh$, and $\\sup\\|F^{(18)}\\|\\leq M$, then $$\\label{holo:interpolation-bound}\n \\left\\|\\frac{F''(0)}2\\right\\|\n       \\leq K_1\\frac b h+K_2 M h^{16},\\qquad\n K_1=\\sum_{j=1}^{17}|c_j|j,\\quad\n K_2=\\frac1{18!}\\sum_{j=1}^{17}|c_j|j^{18}.$$ Both constants are finite and independent of $F,h,T$.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-holo-interpolation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F:[0,17h]\\to V$ be $C^{18}$, where $V$ is a finite-dimensional normed vector space and $h>0$.           "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Weighted constraint space and finite seminorm neighborhoods** ([source](#stmt-phase-space)): For smooth symmetric two-tensors $f,j$ set $$\label{phase:seminorms} p_m(f,j)=\sum_{i=0}^m \left\{\sup_\Sigma \rho^{1+i}\|\nabla_*^if\|_* +\sup_\Sigma \rho^{2+i}\|\nabla_*^ij\|_*\right\}, \qquad m=0,1,\ldots.$$ Let $\mathcal D$ consist of smooth pairs $d=(h,K)$ such that $h$ is positive definite and complete, every $p_m(h-h_*,K-K_*)$ is finite, and $$\label{phase:constraints} R(h)+(\operatorname{tr}_hK)^2-\|K\|_h^2=0,\qquad \operatorname{div}_hK-\mathrm d(\operatorname{tr}_hK)=0.$$ The topology on $\mathcal D$ is the relative topology of the seminorms <a href="#phase:seminorms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="phase:seminorms">[phase:seminorms]</a>. For $\varepsilon>0$ let $$\mathcal U_\varepsilon=\{d\in\mathcal D:p_{10}(d-d_*)<\varepsilon\}, \qquad d_*=(h_*,K_*).$$
- **Future extension with differentiable metric** ([source](#stmt-phase-extension)): A future $C^1$ extension of $\mathcal M_d$ is a smooth, time-orientation-preserving isometric embedding $\iota:\mathcal M_d\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal M}$ onto a proper open subset of a connected, time-oriented smooth four-manifold with nondegenerate $C^1$ Lorentzian metric $\widetilde g$, together with a future-directed timelike $C^1$ curve $c:[0,1]\to\widetilde{\mathcal M}$ such that $$c([0,1))\subset\iota(\mathcal M_d),\qquad c(1)\in\partial\iota(\mathcal M_d).$$ Neither the vacuum equations nor global hyperbolicity are imposed on the ambient spacetime.
- **Passing transport test** ([source](#stmt-test-definition)): For $O\in\mathcal O$ and $B\in\mathbb N$, $B\ge1$, the set $\mathcal A(O,B)\subset\mathcal U$ consists of the data such that, for every $w\in O$, one has $T_d(w)\le B$ and, for every $0<\tau<T_d(w)$ and every piecewise smooth loop $\ell$ based at $\gamma_{d,w}(\tau)$ whose entire image is contained in $I^+_{\mathcal M_d}(\Sigma)$, $$\label{test:inequality} L(\ell)<B^{-1} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \lVert P_\ell-\operatorname{Id}\rVert_{k_{d,w}(\tau)} \le B L(\ell).$$
- **Fixed-order subexponential bounds** ([source](#stmt-bg-subexponential)): The notation $Q_T\le e^{o(T)}$ means that, for every $\sigma>0$, $Q_T\le C_\sigma e^{\sigma T}$ for all sufficiently large $T$. The constants may depend on $d$, the fixed coordinate domains, and every fixed derivative and formal expansion order. A positive quantity has two-sided subexponential size if this holds for the quantity and its reciprocal. A finite product or sum of such bounds, including a factor polynomial in $T$, is again subexponential. No uniformity over unbounded derivative orders is intended.
- **Initial seed and maximal unit timelike geodesic** ([source](#test:seed)): A seed $w\in T\Sigma$ includes its base point. For $d=(h,K)$ let $n$ be the future unit normal to the initial hypersurface in $\mathcal M_d$, and define $$\label{test:seed} U_d(w)=\sqrt{1+h(w,w)}\,n+w, \qquad \gamma_{d,w}:[0,T_d(w))\longrightarrow\mathcal M_d .$$ Here $\gamma_{d,w}$ is the maximal future unit timelike geodesic with this initial velocity, and $T_d(w)\in(0,\infty]$ is its maximal proper duration in the *full* maximal globally hyperbolic development. On $\mathbb R n\oplus T\Sigma$ put $$\label{test:positive-metric} k_{d,w}(0)(sn+X,tn+Y)=st+h(X,Y), \qquad \nabla_{\dot\gamma_{d,w}}k_{d,w}=0.$$ Thus $k_{d,w}(\tau)$ is positive definite even though the spacetime metric is Lorentzian. All operator norms below are induced by this positive inner product.
- **Transported positive metric** ([source](#test:positive-metric)): A seed $w\in T\Sigma$ includes its base point. For $d=(h,K)$ let $n$ be the future unit normal to the initial hypersurface in $\mathcal M_d$, and define $$\label{test:seed} U_d(w)=\sqrt{1+h(w,w)}\,n+w, \qquad \gamma_{d,w}:[0,T_d(w))\longrightarrow\mathcal M_d .$$ Here $\gamma_{d,w}$ is the maximal future unit timelike geodesic with this initial velocity, and $T_d(w)\in(0,\infty]$ is its maximal proper duration in the *full* maximal globally hyperbolic development. On $\mathbb R n\oplus T\Sigma$ put $$\label{test:positive-metric} k_{d,w}(0)(sn+X,tn+Y)=st+h(X,Y), \qquad \nabla_{\dot\gamma_{d,w}}k_{d,w}=0.$$ Thus $k_{d,w}(\tau)$ is positive definite even though the spacetime metric is Lorentzian. All operator norms below are induced by this positive inner product.
- **Developed length and based transport operator** ([source](#test:developed-length)): For a piecewise smooth loop $\ell:[0,1]\to\mathcal M_d$ based at $p=\gamma_{d,w}(\tau)$, parallel transport $k_{d,w}(\tau)$ along $\ell$ and call the resulting positive inner products $k_\ell(s)$. Define the developed length and based transport operator by $$\label{test:developed-length} L(\ell)=\int_0^1\|\dot\ell(s)\|_{k_\ell(s)}\,ds, \qquad P_\ell:T_p\mathcal M_d\longrightarrow T_p\mathcal M_d.$$ If $F(s)$ is the parallel frame along $\ell$ starting from a $k_{d,w}(\tau)$-orthonormal frame, and $\dot\ell=Fz$, then $L(\ell)=\int_0^1\|z(s)\|\,ds$. The metric at the end of the loop need not equal its initial value; Equation <a href="#test:developed-length" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="test:developed-length">[test:developed-length]</a> uses the transported metric at each parameter value.
- **Full entry-jet norm** ([source](#prep:entry-norm)): $$\label{prep:entry-norm} \mathfrak J_{r,T}(W)= \sum_{\|I\|\le r}\left( \left\\|D^I\widehat W\big\|_{\mathcal S_T}\right\\|_{L^\infty} +\left\\|D^I\widehat W\big\|_{\mathcal S_T}\right\\|_{L^2(dx\,d\operatorname{vol}_\gamma)} \right).$$ The sum uses a fixed finite angular atlas and includes ordinary spacetime derivatives, including derivatives normal to $\mathcal S$. It is not a norm of tangential jets alone. We also write $\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F)$ when $F$ is already the normalized array. The entry interval has length $O(T)$ and uniformly controlled angular volume, so its $L^2$ part costs at most a polynomial factor relative to the corresponding uniform jet bound.

      "exact_definition": "$$\\label{prep:entry-norm}\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(W)=\n \\sum_{|I|\\le r}\\left(\n   \\left\\|D^I\\widehat W\\big|_{\\mathcal S_T}\\right\\|_{L^\\infty}\n  +\\left\\|D^I\\widehat W\\big|_{\\mathcal S_T}\\right\\|_{L^2(dx\\,d\\operatorname{vol}_\\gamma)}\n                         \\right).$$ The sum uses a fixed finite angular atlas and includes ordinary spacetime derivatives, including derivatives normal to $\\mathcal S$. It is not a norm of tangential jets alone. We also write $\\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F)$ when $F$ is already the normalized array. The entry interval has length $O(T)$ and uniformly controlled angular volume, so its $L^2$ part costs at most a polynomial factor relative to the corresponding uniform jet bound.",
      "interpretation": "The entry norm combines uniform and slice integral norms of ordinary spacetime derivatives of normalized arrays, including derivatives normal to the entry cylinder.",
      "kind": "notation",
      "name": "Full entry-jet norm",
      "scope": "Finite entry intervals and their spacetime collars."
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-264.2",
  "schema": "pm.objects.v2"
}

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-observer-comparison-factor | c(w) | = | $\bigl(\sqrt{1+h(w,w)}+\sqrt{h(w,w)}\bigr)^2$ | A fixed datum and seed, or a sufficiently small neighborhood of that pair. |
| q-packet-amplitude-decay | \alpha | = | $7\kappa/8$ | Fixed output orders and fixed positive frequency exponent before inverse accuracy is prescribed. |
| q-packet-frequency | \lambda_T | = | $e^{\zeta T}$ | Fixed frequency exponent and sufficiently large optical depth after all finite construction orders are chosen. |
| q-interior-packet-jet-bound | \\|q_T\\|_{C^N(\mathcal D_T)} | ≤ | $A_T\lambda_T^N e^{o(T)}$ | Each specified finite derivative order; constants may depend on fixed formal degrees. |
| q-exterior-beam-jet-bound | \\|h_T^B\\|_{C^{N_b}} | ≤ | $A_T\lambda_T^{N_b+3/2}e^{o(T)}$ | Fixed exterior base buffer; increasing inverse accuracy does not increase its positive frequency power. |
| q-protected-ball-radius | \rho_T | = | $(1-2\beta)t_T$ | Fixed sufficiently small localization tolerance and sufficiently large parameters in the packet sequence. |
| q-individual-entry-error | \mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^\pm) | ≤ | $A_T^2e^{\eta T}$ | Prescribed finite data and entry orders, loss and late-support fraction. |
| q-odd-entry-error | \mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^+-F_T^-) | ≤ | $A_T^3e^{\eta T}$ | Fixed finite entry order and prescribed positive loss. |
| q-interior-loss-budget | \delta | < | $\kappa/32$ | Positive fixed loss selected before frequency exponent and formal accuracy. |
| q-individual-interior-error | \lVert F_T^\pm\rVert_{C^{30}_{\mathrm{mix}}(\Omega_T)} | ≤ | $e^{(\kappa-2\alpha+\delta)T}$ | Fixed derivative orders and sufficiently small frequency exponent followed by sufficiently large optical depth. |
| q-total-relative-change | \lVert H_T^\pm\rVert_{C^{30}_{\mathrm{mix}}} | ≤ | $e^{-(\kappa/2+c)T}$ | Fixed finite orders and a positive decay margin after allocating losses. |
| q-integrated-common-energy-rate | \mathcal R_T(L) | ≤ | $(\kappa+\eta)T$ | Fixed finite energy orders and prescribed small positive rate tolerance. |
| q-loop-parameter | h_T | = | $e^{-\kappa T/16}$ | The selected sign and sufficiently large optical depth. |
| q-loop-developed-length | \mathcal L_T(h) | ≤ | $a_phQ_T$ | The complete loop parameter interval stated in the lemma. |
| q-holonomy-derivative-bound | \sup_{0\leq h\leq17h_T}\\|\mathcal H_T^{(18)}(h)\\| | ≤ | $Q_T$ | Fixed finite derivative order over the stated parameter interval. |
| q-interpolation-value-constant | K_1 | = | $\sum_{j=1}^{17}\|c_j\|j$ | Independent of the function, sampling scale and optical parameter. |
| q-interpolation-remainder-constant | K_2 | = | $\frac1{18!}\sum_{j=1}^{17}\|c_j\|j^{18}$ | Independent of the function, sampling scale and optical parameter. |

          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite data order $m\\ge10$, a finite entry order $r$, a loss $\\eta>0$, and $0<\\nu_0<1$. After fixing all the finite derivative orders needed below, one can choose $\\zeta>0$ and then the finite formal accuracy orders in Proposition <a href=\"#bg:packet\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet\">21</a> so that the following hold for all sufficiently large parameters $T$ in the packet sequence.*\n\n\n*There are smooth positive complete vacuum data $d_T^\\pm\\in\\mathcal D$ on the whole bridge, with $$\\label{prep:data-convergence}\n p_m(d_T^\\pm-d)\\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular $d_T^\\pm\\in\\mathcal U$ for large $T$. They agree exactly with $d$ on the other end, the connecting region, and a protected region containing every fixed compact subset for large $T$. On the selected asymptotic end the protected ball is $$\\label{prep:protected-ball}\n B_T=\\{|z-z_*|<\\rho_T\\},\\qquad\n z_*=C_e(t_T),\\qquad \\rho_T=(1-2\\beta)t_T,$$ where $\\beta>0$ is fixed sufficiently small. The data have the weight-one metric and weight-two second-form symbol bounds at every order. Their full correction tails are retained; asymptotic coefficients are allowed to change.*\n\n\n*Ordinary exterior vacuum evolutions $g_T^\\pm$, in exact wave-map gauge with the same target $g$, exist on the finite domains needed to reach $\\mathcal S_T$, with fixed overlap margins and strict future-outflow artificial boundaries. Set $$H_T^\\pm=\\widehat{g_T^\\pm-g},\\qquad\n F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T\n \\quad\\hbox{on the entry collar}.$$ Then $$\\label{prep:entry-estimates}\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^\\pm)\\le A_T^2e^{\\eta T},\\qquad\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^+-F_T^-)\\le A_T^3e^{\\eta T}.$$ The exact changes, packet, and errors vanish on the earlier needed entry interval $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$. In particular, $r=61$ is permitted, with an arbitrarily small prescribed loss. The scaled linear deep residual can simultaneously be at most $e^{-4\\kappa T}$ through order $60$, or at any other finite orders fixed in advance. The same statement holds for the separately prepared exchanged branch.",

After fixing all the finite derivative orders needed below, one can choose $\\zeta>0$ and then the finite formal accuracy orders in Proposition <a href=\"#bg:packet\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet\">21</a> so that the following hold for all sufficiently large parameters $T$ in the packet sequence.*\n\n\n*There are smooth positive complete vacuum data $d_T^\\pm\\in\\mathcal D$ on the whole bridge, with $$\\label{prep:data-convergence}\n p_m(d_T^\\pm-d)\\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular $d_T^\\pm\\in\\mathcal U$ for large $T$. They agree exactly with $d$ on the other end, the connecting region, and a protected region containing every fixed compact subset for large $T$. On the selected asymptotic end the protected ball is $$\\label{prep:protected-ball}\n B_T=\\{|z-z_*|<\\rho_T\\},\\qquad\n z_*=C_e(t_T),\\qquad \\rho_T=(1-2\\beta)t_T,$$ where $\\beta>0$ is fixed sufficiently small. The data have the weight-one metric and weight-two second-form symbol bounds at every order. Their full correction tails are retained; asymptotic coefficients are allowed to change.*\n\n\n*Ordinary exterior vacuum evolutions $g_T^\\pm$, in exact wave-map gauge with the same target $g$, exist on the finite domains needed to reach $\\mathcal S_T$, with fixed overlap margins and strict future-outflow artificial boundaries. Set $$H_T^\\pm=\\widehat{g_T^\\pm-g},\\qquad\n F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T\n \\quad\\hbox{on the entry collar}.$$ Then $$\\label{prep:entry-estimates}\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^\\pm)\\le A_T^2e^{\\eta T},\\qquad\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^+-F_T^-)\\le A_T^3e^{\\eta T}.$$ The exact changes, packet, and errors vanish on the earlier needed entry interval $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$. In particular, $r=61$ is permitted, with an arbitrarily small prescribed loss. The scaled linear deep residual can simultaneously be at most $e^{-4\\kappa T}$ through order $60$, or at any other finite orders fixed in advance. The same statement holds for the separately prepared exchanged branch.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-energy-signed-experiment",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\delta<\\kappa/32$ and the prescribed finite data seminorm. Use Proposition <a href=\"#bg:packet\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet\">21</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prep:signed-entry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prep:signed-entry\">24</a> with sufficiently high fixed orders, sufficiently small losses and late support parameter, and sufficiently accurate linear residuals. For sufficiently small fixed $\\zeta>0$ and sufficiently large $T$, the two entry data have smooth vacuum evolutions $g_T^\\pm$ on the strict domain <a href=\"#energy:domain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"energy:domain\">[energy:domain]</a>, in wave-map gauge with target $g$. Let $H_T^\\pm$ be the relative arrays of $g_T^\\pm-g$, and put $$F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T,\\qquad\n F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}=F_T^+-F_T^-.$$ Then $$\\label{energy:conclusion}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert F_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^{30}_{\\mathrm{mix}}(\\Omega_T)}\n   &\\le e^{(\\kappa-2\\alpha+\\delta)T},\\\\\n \\lVert F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}(\\Omega_T)}\n   &\\le e^{(\\kappa-3\\alpha+\\delta)T}\n            +e^{(2\\kappa-4\\alpha+\\delta)T}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The exact changes and all three error fields have support in $v\\ge(1-\\nu_0)T-o(1)$. The time slices remain spacelike and the two moving side faces remain strict future exits. Moreover, for some $c>0$, $$\\label{energy:small-relative}\n \\lVert H_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^{30}_{\\mathrm{mix}}}\n       \\le e^{-(\\kappa/2+c)T},\\qquad\n e^{o(T)}\\left(\\lVert F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}}\n          +\\sum_\\pm\\lVert H_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}}^2\\right)\n       =o(A_T).$$ Here every subexponential factor is at a fixed finite order. No higher seminorm of the fixed smooth background is required to be small.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],

        {
          "anchor": "energy:rate-integral",
          "exact_quote": "Here is an explicit common rate construction. Fix a desired small $\\eta>0$. Choose $\\varepsilon_{\\mathrm m}$, then a bulk threshold $B_0$, so that all same-level square-root rates at the finitely many used orders are at most $2\\kappa+\\eta_0$ on $\\min(t,v,-u)\\ge B_0$, where $\\eta_0$ is as small as needed. Their rates elsewhere are bounded by a constant $C_0$. These constants apply simultaneously to both actual metrics and the background operator; enlarge $T$ to absorb <a href=\"#energy:nonlinear-small-rate\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"energy:nonlinear-small-rate\">[energy:nonlinear-small-rate]</a>. On the support <a href=\"#energy:known-support\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"energy:known-support\">[energy:known-support]</a>, a point outside the bulk can occur only at times in $$J_T=[0,B_0]\\ \\cup\\\n \\left[\\frac{(1-\\nu_0)T-\\omega_T-B_0}{2},L\\right].$$ Indeed $v\\ge B_0$ for large $T$, and $-u=v-2t$. In particular $|J_T|\\le\\nu_0T/2+O(B_0+1)$. Choose $\\nu_0$ after $C_0$, sufficiently small, and set for example $$\\label{energy:common-rate}\n r_T(t)=2\\kappa+\\eta_0+C_0\\mathbf1_{J_T}(t),\\qquad\n \\mathcal R_T(t)=\\int_0^t r_T(s)\\,ds.$$ Increasing $C_0$ if necessary and making the preceding choices strict gives $$\\label{energy:rate-integral}\n \\mathcal R_T(L)\\le(\\kappa+\\eta)T.$$ It is a single nonnegative pointwise majorant for both individual equations and the background linear equation at every used order, not a terminal estimate chosen separately at each level.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-integrated-common-energy-rate",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "\\mathcal R_T(L)",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "The common nonnegative rate for both signed equations and the background linear equation.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "energy:hierarchy",
            "exact_quote": "Combining the source and commutator bounds yields, in integrated form or almost everywhere after regularization, $$\\label{energy:hierarchy}\n E_j'\\le r_T E_j+\\sum_{\\ell<j}a_{j\\ell}(t)E_\\ell+S_j(t),\n \\qquad a_{j\\ell}\\ge0,\\qquad\n \\int_0^L a_{j\\ell}(t)\\,dt\\le e^{o(T)}.$$ At a zero of an energy this follows by first using $(\\mathcal E_j+\\epsilon^2)^{1/2}$ and then letting $\\epsilon\\downarrow0$. Same-level couplings among entries and multiindices were summed within $\\mathcal E_j$. The remaining matrix in <a href=\"#energy:hierarchy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"energy:hierarchy\">[energy:hierarchy]</a> strictly lowers the ordinary commutation order.",
            "source_id": "PM-264.2"
          }
        ],
        "parameter_scope": "Fixed finite energy orders and prescribed small positive rate tolerance.",
        "quantifiers": []

      "relation": "≤",

    },

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "holo:h-choice",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{holo:h-choice}\n h_T=e^{-\\kappa T/16}.$$ For $0\\leq h\\leq17h_T$, let $$\\label{holo:surface}\n \\Psi_h(s,t)=\\operatorname{Fl}^{1}_{h(sf_1+tf_2)}(p_T),\n                  \\qquad (s,t)\\in[0,1]^2.$$ The vector field in this expression depends on the parameters $s,t,h$, and $\\operatorname{Fl}^1$ is its actual time-one flow. Let $\\ell_h$ be the image of the positively oriented square boundary, based at $(0,0)$, and let $\\mathcal H_T(h)$ be its parallel transport matrix in $E(p_T)$. Write $\\mathcal L_T(h)$ for its length in the positive metric obtained by continuing the test’s parallel metric along the loop from $p_T$.",

        }
