<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-baire-phase-space** [claim](#stmt-phase-baire) / [proof](#proof-stmt-phase-baire): Weighted smooth completeness, openness of positivity and closedness of the constraint zero locus. -> A completely metrizable constraint space and finite seminorm neighborhood base.
- **step-observer-norm-comparison** [claim](#stmt-test-observer-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-observer-comparison): Diagonalize the initial positive observer metric and preserve its inequalities by parallel transport. -> Uniform norm comparison near a fixed datum and seed.
- **step-closed-tests** [claim](#stmt-test-closed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-closed): Persist strict compact duration and loop witnesses using Cauchy stability and smooth differential equation dependence. -> Each passing set is relatively closed.
- **step-causal-diamond-homotopy** [claim](#stmt-test-diamond) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-diamond): Use a compact intrinsic causal diamond to make the admissible homotopy parameter set open and closed. -> Every homotopy path remains intrinsic.
- **step-interior-endpoint-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-test-interior-endpoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-interior-endpoint): Temporal-chart geodesic estimates give a limiting tangent and smooth continuation. -> No interior endpoint and a past crossing of the Cauchy hypersurface.
- **step-developed-loop-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-test-loop-bootstrap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-loop-bootstrap): Integrate frame growth in accumulated developed length and rule out first chart departure. -> A uniform linear transport bound for arbitrary short developed-length loops.
- **step-extension-exit-test-cover** [claim](#stmt-test-exit-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-exit-cover): Construct a timelike straight exit, an open intrinsic geodesic family and common chart transport bounds. -> The extending datum belongs to a passing test.
- **step-comparison-runout-classification** [claim](#stmt-test-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-comparison): Apply the complete comparison construction, exterior completeness and monotonicity of optical coordinates. -> An embedded comparison wedge and branch-or-corner first-runout classification.
- **step-individual-corner-track-control** [claim](#stmt-test-corner-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-corner-bounds): Integrate logarithmic momentum variation using null connection identities and finite optical variation. -> Track-dependent optical-speed and screen-velocity bounds.
- **step-corner-nullity** [claim](#stmt-test-corner-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-corner-null): Preserve canonical section volume under variable-time hitting maps and use shrinking strips with Fatou's inequality. -> Corner seeds are contained in a null set.
- **step-select-branch-seed** [claim](#stmt-test-branch-seed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-test-branch-seed): Exclude an all-corner open family and reprepare optical labels on the same spacetime. -> A branch observer in the original seed set.
- **step-geometric-background-interface** [claim](#stmt-bg-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bg-geometry): Import prepared exterior and cylinder estimates, exhaust finite diamonds, then establish expanding-domain subexponential jets. -> Full-wedge low geometry and fixed-order coefficient control.
- **step-branch-normalization** [claim](#stmt-bg-branch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bg-branch): Integrate momentum variation and apply Hamiltonian Gronwall in the finite optical coordinate. -> Bounded branch state and observation lapse scale without curvature assumptions.
- **step-exterior-rays-and-flow** [claim](#stmt-bg-exterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bg-exterior): Verify strict radial escape for prepared launch covectors and integrate far-region connection and variation bounds. -> Bridge return shell, transverse entry and subexponential flow jets.
- **step-normalized-coefficient-analysis** [claim](#stmt-bg-coefficients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bg-coefficients): Compute normalized connections, brackets and curvature, retaining constant tensor-slot products. -> Bounded scaled wave coefficients with the correct limiting radial operator.
- **step-constrained-packet-and-beam** [claim](#stmt-bg-packet) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bg-packet): Solve constrained triangular transports, propagate complex beam jets through caustics and match full entry jets with finite remainder bounds. -> A polarized interior packet and exterior beam with controlled residuals and support.
- **step-linear-amplitude-rescaling** [claim](#stmt-bg-amplitude-independent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bg-amplitude-independent): Multiply the homogeneous linear construction by the chosen exponential amplitude. -> Linear conclusions at any fixed positive exponential amplitude decay rate.
- **step-shared-constraint-correction** [claim](#stmt-prep-constraint-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-constraint-pair): Use a common support-preserving contraction and regularize the same fixed points by rotations and dilations. -> Smooth signed corrections with controlled odd difference and retained tails.
- **step-signed-data-and-entry** [claim](#stmt-prep-signed-entry) / [proof](#pm-h-24): Rescale beam seeds, correct constraints, initialize exact gauge jets and propagate individual and odd exterior errors with one small common rate. -> Nearby complete vacuum data and quadratic individual, cubic odd entry errors with exact early agreement.
- **step-general-amplitude-exterior** [claim](#stmt-prep-amplitude-independent-exterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-amplitude-independent-exterior): Allocate amplitude-dependent losses, reduce fixed frequency powers and raise inverse accuracy after fixing the exterior rate. -> General-amplitude signed conclusions through the entry cylinder only.
- **step-volterra-interval-propagator** [claim](#stmt-energy-volterra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-energy-volterra): Conjugate by the common integrated rate and terminate the time-ordered expansion using strict commutation-level descents. -> A subexponential propagator uniform in both time endpoints.
- **step-signed-interior-propagation** [claim](#stmt-energy-signed-experiment) / [proof](#pm-h-32): Retain the exact principal operator, close individual massive flux energies and estimate odd forcing with higher mass buffers and intermediate-time propagation. -> Finite vacuum interior evolutions with strict outflow and signed relative-error separation.
- **step-whole-data-attachment** [claim](#stmt-attach-development) / [proof](#proof-stmt-attach-development): Identify actual reduced solutions on collars, retain past-preserved strict domain gaps and prove the complete bridge is Cauchy before maximal embedding. -> The finite observation lies in the full maximal development of the corrected datum.
- **step-fixed-canonical-tube** [claim](#stmt-attach-canonical-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-attach-canonical-tube): Bound shifted momentum on a fixed state neighborhood and retain lapse factors in differentiated background Hamiltonian equations. -> A fixed state tube with an integrable background Lipschitz rate.
- **step-absolute-shell-root** [claim](#stmt-attach-absolute-shell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-attach-absolute-shell): Multiply the unit shell by the lapse and apply a uniform simple-root argument in absolute canonical momentum. -> A future perturbed root and controlled absolute geodesic vector-field difference.
- **step-adaptive-observer-return** [claim](#stmt-attach-observer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-attach-observer): Prescribe the isometric terminal tangent, integrate backwards with the background integrable Lipschitz rate and return through unchanged compact collars. -> Nearby initial seeds reaching the observation with uniform test-frame comparison.
- **step-scaled-curvature-taylor** [claim](#stmt-holo-curvature-functional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holo-curvature-functional): Express scaled curvature as a smooth relative-jet functional and subtract signed Taylor expansions. -> The signed curvature difference equals its linear packet contribution plus a negligible remainder.
- **step-signed-signal-separation** [claim](#stmt-holo-signal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holo-signal): Read the leading phase derivatives on the polarized angular entry and dominate lower-order terms. -> At least one signed curvature observation exceeds the amplitude.
- **step-boosted-connection-control** [claim](#stmt-holo-boosted-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holo-boosted-control): Enumerate negative boost exponents and use exact null compatibility or vanishing background entries with small perturbations. -> Subexponential fixed-order connection, frame and bracket jets.
- **step-loop-flow-and-holonomy** [claim](#stmt-holo-loops) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holo-loops): Differentiate actual parameter-dependent flow, frame variation and parallel transport equations; identify curvature by the pulled-back connection. -> Short developed-length loops, controlled holonomy derivatives and the quadratic curvature coefficient.
- **step-exact-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-holo-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holo-interpolation): Extract the quadratic Lagrange coefficients, prove their moment identities and apply Taylor's integral remainder. -> Exact extraction identity and value-plus-remainder bound.
- **step-destroy-passing-test** [claim](#stmt-holo-violation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holo-violation): Assume the selected signed datum still passes, apply the transport bound to sampled loops and contradict the signed curvature signal using exact interpolation. -> A nearby signed datum outside the same passing test.
- **step-baire-generic-inextendibility** [claim](#stmt-phase-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holo-violation-2): Show every closed passing set has empty relative interior, intersect their countably many open dense complements and undo mass normalization. -> A dense residual subset with no future extension in the specified sense.
- **step-trivial-spatial-data-isometries** [claim](#stmt-phase-trivial-isometries) / [proof](#proof-stmt-phase-trivial-isometries): Use compact spacelike normal graphs whose second-form trace has a distinguished maximum, simple Hessian and nonzero odd directional derivatives. -> Nearby complete vacuum data with trivial spatial data isometry group.

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-bg-geometry",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bg-geometry",
          "exact_quote": "There is a choice of $\\epsilon>0$, depending only on the fixed Kerr center and the fixed preparation, such that every $p_{10}(d-d_*)<\\epsilon$ in the stated smooth symbol class has the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The cylinder $\\mathcal S$ in Proposition <a href=\"#test:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"test:comparison\">12</a> has a smooth vacuum development on the full product wedge $[0,\\infty)_t\\times\\mathbb R_x\\times\\mathbb S^2$ in Equation <a href=\"#bg:wedge\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:wedge\">[bg:wedge]</a>. The quantities $\\mathcal A^{\\pm1},\\gamma^{\\pm1}$ are uniformly bounded in angular $H^6$, and $\\xi_i,a^{-1}\\partial_vb$ in angular $H^5$. There are nonnegative functions $g_i(y_i)$ with summable unit-cell suprema such that, in the indicated low angular norms, $$\\label{bg:envelopes}\n     \\|\\chi_i,\\ell_i+\\kappa\\|_{H^4}\\le C(q+g_i(y_i)),\n     \\qquad \\|b\\|_{H^5}\\le C(q+g_0(u)).$$*\n\n\n2.  *The following quantities are uniformly bounded, and tend to zero when $t,|u|,|v|\\to\\infty$: $$\\label{bg:low-limits}\n    \\begin{gathered}\n     \\|b\\|_{H^5}+\\sum_i\\|\\chi_i,\\ell_i+\\kappa\\|_{H^4},\\\\\n     \\sum_{k=0}^1\\left(\\|\\partial_{y_k}b\\|_{H^2}\n           +\\sum_i\\|\\partial_{y_k}\\chi_i,\n                              \\partial_{y_k}\\ell_i\\|_{H^2}\\right),\\\\\n     \\sum_{k=0}^1\\left(\\|\\partial_{y_k}\\gamma\\|_{H^4}\n                     +\\|\\partial_{y_k}\\log a+\\kappa\\|_{H^4}\\right).\n    \\end{gathered}$$ In particular $D\\log a,D\\gamma,b,Db$ are uniformly bounded.*\n\n\n\n3.  *For each fixed $U\\in\\mathbb R$, $C>0$, and finite order, all ordinary derivatives of $$\\label{bg:high-arrays}\n     \\gamma^{\\pm1},\\quad\\log\\mathcal A,\\quad b,\n                      \\quad a^{-1}\\partial_vb$$ are bounded by $e^{o(T)}$ on $$\\label{bg:truncation}\n     t\\ge0,\\qquad y_d\\le U,\\qquad y_l\\le CT,\n     \\qquad \\{d,l\\}=\\{0,1\\}.$$ This includes every restriction and coordinate-path subsegment. Every fixed derivative of $a$, $\\sqrt a$, or $\\partial_vb$ retains, respectively, the factor $a$, $\\sqrt a$, or $a$.*\n\n4.  *Either branch can be put in the convention $v\\to\\infty$, $u\\to u_*<\\infty$. Exchanging the optical labels and preparing the angular coordinates anew preserves all these conclusions on the same product spacetime and initial cylinder.*\n\n*Only $p_{10}$ is required small; no higher seminorm, radiation coefficient, or curvature measured by a timelike observer is required small or bounded.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-geometric-background-interface",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed Kerr center and preparation",
        "Small defining seminorm",
        "Finite higher symbol seminorms"

      "method": "Import prepared exterior and cylinder estimates, exhaust finite diamonds, then establish expanding-domain subexponential jets.",
      "output": "Full-wedge low geometry and fixed-order coefficient control.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-bg-branch",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bg-branch",
          "exact_quote": "Let a future unit timelike geodesic approach a branch $v\\to\\infty$, $u\\to u_*<\\infty$, in the comparison wedge. With proper time denoted by $\\tau$, put $$p=a\\dot v,\\qquad \\Xi=\\dot\\theta-b\\dot u,\\qquad P=\\gamma\\Xi.$$ Then $p\\asymp1$, and $P$, $\\Xi$, and $\\dot u$ stay bounded on a late segment. The angular position has a limit. At the point $p_T$ with $v=T$ one has $$\\label{bg:branch-scales}\n             a_p:=a(p_T)\\asymp e^{-\\kappa T},\n             \\qquad \\dot v(p_T)\\asymp e^{\\kappa T}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-branch-normalization",
      "inputs": [
        "Selected branch observer",
        "Low background geometry"

      "method": "Integrate momentum variation and apply Hamiltonian Gronwall in the finite optical coordinate.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-bg-branch"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-bg-exterior",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-geometric-background-interface"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bg-exterior",
          "exact_quote": "On either prepared end use regular exterior time $t_e$, co-moving coordinates $y$, and lab coordinates $z=y+C_e(t_e)$, with fixed $\\sup|C_e'|\\le s_*<1/10$, after decreasing $\\epsilon$ if needed. The relevant slice $t_e=0$ agrees with the original bridge sufficiently far out. There is a preparation parameter $I$ with $0<c_I\\le I'\\le C_I$ and $I(t_T)=T$. The launch patch near $v=x=T$ on $\\mathcal S$ occurs at $t_e=t_T+O(1)$, and $t_T\\asymp T$. A fixed enlargement is contained in $t_e\\le t_T+C_2$. Its selected-end portion $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$ is earlier by at least $c\\nu_0T-O(1)$ for each fixed $\\nu_0>0$.*\n\n*The exterior metric has uniformly regular spacelike temporal levels. Its first and second ordinary lab derivatives are bounded on the computing region and become arbitrarily small at sufficiently large co-radius, uniformly in time. Every fixed mixed jet on the used time and radius ranges $O(1+T)$ is $e^{o(T)}$. Entry charts have a noncharacteristic collar with controlled low jets and subexponential fixed higher jets; the inner exit has a strict margin deeper than the entry cylinder.*\n\n\n*The null covectors $dv$ launched on a sufficiently small fixed patch at this entry escape towards the past to the same original end. For each fixed $\\beta>0$, after fixing a sufficiently large radius and taking $T$ large, their bridge arrival points satisfy $$\\label{bg:arrival-shell}\n (1-\\beta)t_T<|z-z_*|<(1+\\beta)t_T,\n                  \\qquad z_*=C_e(t_T).$$ The entry crossing is unique and transverse, and separated from other used ray portions outside a uniform collar. Every fixed Hamiltonian flow jet, including launch-center derivatives and the propagators on all subsegments, is $e^{o(T)}$. Tensor parallel transport and its inverse are bounded. These assertions hold on short past and future launch enlargements through the deeper exit.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-exterior-rays-and-flow",
      "inputs": [
        "Prepared exterior geometry",
        "Fixed profile depth",
        "Small launch patch"
      ],
      "method": "Verify strict radial escape for prepared launch covectors and integrate far-region connection and variation bounds.",
      "output": "Bridge return shell, transverse entry and subexponential flow jets.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-bg-exterior"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-bg-coefficients",
      "depends_on": [

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bg-coefficients",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V_\\alpha$ be the matrix of $\\nabla_{Z_\\alpha}$ on the normalized vector basis. These matrices are bounded, and in the bulk limit $\\min(v,-u,t)\\to\\infty$, $$\\label{bg:connection-limits}\n V_0\\longrightarrow D_0=\\operatorname{diag}(-\\kappa/2,\\kappa/2,0,0),\n \\qquad V_1\\longrightarrow-D_0,\\qquad V_A\\longrightarrow0.$$ The differentiated connection terms contracted in the tensor operator $a\\square_g$, and every normalized entry of $a\\operatorname{Riem}(g)$, are bounded and tend to zero in this limit. Their fixed ordinary jets and those of the brackets of $Z$ are $e^{o(T)}$ on Equation <a href=\"#bg:truncation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:truncation\">[bg:truncation]</a>. Moreover $\\nabla_{L_1}L_1=\\ell_1L_1$ exactly. The undifferentiated constant connection products are retained: on entry $n$ the limiting radial operator is $$\\label{bg:radial-conjugation}\n\\begin{split}\n &-(L_0+c_n)(L_1-c_n)-(L_1-c_n)(L_0+c_n)\\\\\n &\\qquad=-L_0L_1-L_1L_0+2c_n(L_0-L_1)+2c_n^2.\n\\end{split}$$ It is the radial part of $e^{2c_nx}a\\square_g^{\\rm scal}e^{-2c_nx}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-normalized-coefficient-analysis",
      "inputs": [
        "Normalized null and angular frame",
        "Low and high geometric estimates"
      ],
      "method": "Compute normalized connections, brackets and curvature, retaining constant tensor-slot products.",
      "output": "Bounded scaled wave coefficients with the correct limiting radial operator.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-bg-coefficients"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-bg-packet",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-branch-normalization",
        "step-exterior-rays-and-flow",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bg-packet",
          "exact_quote": "Fix any finite list of output derivative orders, including an exterior base buffer $N_b$. For every fixed $\\zeta>0$ and every subsequently prescribed inverse accuracy $M_1$, sufficiently high finite transport and Taylor orders yield a real interior tensor $h_T^p$ and a real exterior tensor $h_T^B$ with the following properties. Put $$\\label{bg:packet-scales}\n       A_T=e^{-\\alpha T},\\qquad \\alpha=7\\kappa/8,\n       \\qquad\\lambda_T=e^{\\zeta T}.$$ The interior field has the form $$\\label{bg:packet-form}\n h_T^p=A_T\\operatorname{tr.rev.}_g\\operatorname{Re}\n       \\left(e^{i\\lambda_Tv}\\sum_{j=0}^{J}\\lambda_T^{-j}p_j\\right).$$ Every fixed normalized coefficient jet of $p_j$ is $e^{o(T)}$. The field is supported in a fixed-width neighborhood of $v=T$, with angular support in the transported launch patch. Its relative array $q_T=(h_T^p(\\hat e_\\alpha,\\hat e_\\beta))$ satisfies $$\\label{bg:packet-size}\n              \\|q_T\\|_{C^N(\\mathcal D_T)}\n                    \\le A_T\\lambda_T^N e^{o(T)}$$ at each specified order. The tensor $p_0$ is trace-free and $p_0(L_0,\\cdot)=0$. There is a purely angular $\\gamma$-unit vector $X$ at $p_T$ for which $$\\label{bg:polarization}\n \\sigma_T:=\\left|\\operatorname{Re}\n        \\bigl(e^{i\\lambda_TT}p_0(X,X)\\bigr)\\right|$$ has two-sided subexponential size. The $a$-scaled linear wave residual and $\\sqrt a$-scaled linear gauge residual, in normalized slots and with all prescribed ordinary derivatives, are bounded by $A_T\\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$.*\n\n\n*In regular exterior lab components, $$\\label{bg:beam-estimates}\n \\|h_T^B\\|_{C^{N_b}}\\le A_T\\lambda_T^{N_b+3/2}e^{o(T)}.$$ Its linear wave and gauge residuals, and its difference from $h_T^p$ on the entry collar, have size $A_T\\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$ at all the prescribed finite orders. The latter assertion includes normal spacetime jets. On the original bridge its support lies in Equation <a href=\"#bg:arrival-shell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:arrival-shell\">[bg:arrival-shell]</a> with arbitrarily thin tube margins. Increasing $M_1$ and the finite formal degrees does not increase the positive frequency powers in Equations <a href=\"#bg:packet-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet-size\">[bg:packet-size]</a> and <a href=\"#bg:beam-estimates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:beam-estimates\">[bg:beam-estimates]</a>. Thresholds and subexponential constants may depend on those degrees.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],

      "output": "A polarized interior packet and exterior beam with controlled residuals and support.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-bg-packet"

The branch observer needs no bounded-curvature hypothesis.",

      "inputs": [

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prep-constraint-pair",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prep-constraint-pair",
          "exact_quote": "Let $s\\ge3$. Let $D_\\pm=(\\delta+a_\\pm,b_\\pm)$ be smooth positive data on $\\mathbb R^3$ with symbol orders $-1,-2$ at every order, and suppose $$\\|D_\\pm-(\\delta,0)\\|_{X^s}\\le\\varepsilon_s.$$ Suppose the smooth compact sources $\\phi_\\pm$ are supported in $\\{|y'|\\ge1\\}$ and equal $\\Phi(D_\\pm)$ there. For sufficiently small $\\varepsilon_s$ and $b_2:=\\max_\\pm\\|\\phi_\\pm\\|_{Y^s}$ there are corrections $v_\\pm$ supported in $\\{|y'|\\ge1\\}$ such that $$\\Phi(D_\\pm+v_\\pm)=0\\quad (|y'|\\ge1),\\qquad\n \\|v_\\pm\\|_{X^s}\\le C_s b_2.$$ They are obtained with the same $\\mathcal K$ and satisfy $$\\label{prep:abstract-odd-correction}\n \\|v_+-v_-\\|_{X^s}\n \\le C_s\\left(\\|\\phi_+-\\phi_-\\|_{Y^s}\n                  +\\|D_+-D_-\\|_{X^s}b_2\\right).$$ Each correction is smooth across the unit sphere and has symbol bounds of orders $-1,-2$ at every derivative order. Only the stated base norms have to be small. No vacuum equation is required for $D_\\pm$ in the artificial interior $|y'|<1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-shared-constraint-correction",
      "inputs": [
        "Small positive artificial exterior data",
        "Compact exterior constraint defects",
        "Common flat right inverse"

      "method": "Use a common support-preserving contraction and regularize the same fixed points by rotations and dilations.",
      "output": "Smooth signed corrections with controlled odd difference and retained tails.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prep-constraint-pair"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prep-signed-entry",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-constrained-packet-and-beam",
        "step-shared-constraint-correction",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prep-signed-entry",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite data order $m\\ge10$, a finite entry order $r$, a loss $\\eta>0$, and $0<\\nu_0<1$. After fixing all the finite derivative orders needed below, one can choose $\\zeta>0$ and then the finite formal accuracy orders in Proposition <a href=\"#bg:packet\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet\">21</a> so that the following hold for all sufficiently large parameters $T$ in the packet sequence.*\n\n\n*There are smooth positive complete vacuum data $d_T^\\pm\\in\\mathcal D$ on the whole bridge, with $$\\label{prep:data-convergence}\n p_m(d_T^\\pm-d)\\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular $d_T^\\pm\\in\\mathcal U$ for large $T$. They agree exactly with $d$ on the other end, the connecting region, and a protected region containing every fixed compact subset for large $T$. On the selected asymptotic end the protected ball is $$\\label{prep:protected-ball}\n B_T=\\{|z-z_*|<\\rho_T\\},\\qquad\n z_*=C_e(t_T),\\qquad \\rho_T=(1-2\\beta)t_T,$$ where $\\beta>0$ is fixed sufficiently small. The data have the weight-one metric and weight-two second-form symbol bounds at every order. Their full correction tails are retained; asymptotic coefficients are allowed to change.*\n\n\n*Ordinary exterior vacuum evolutions $g_T^\\pm$, in exact wave-map gauge with the same target $g$, exist on the finite domains needed to reach $\\mathcal S_T$, with fixed overlap margins and strict future-outflow artificial boundaries. Set $$H_T^\\pm=\\widehat{g_T^\\pm-g},\\qquad\n F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T\n \\quad\\hbox{on the entry collar}.$$ Then $$\\label{prep:entry-estimates}\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^\\pm)\\le A_T^2e^{\\eta T},\\qquad\n \\mathfrak J_{r,T}(F_T^+-F_T^-)\\le A_T^3e^{\\eta T}.$$ The exact changes, packet, and errors vanish on the earlier needed entry interval $v<(1-\\nu_0)T$. In particular, $r=61$ is permitted, with an arbitrarily small prescribed loss. The scaled linear deep residual can simultaneously be at most $e^{-4\\kappa T}$ through order $60$, or at any other finite orders fixed in advance. The same statement holds for the separately prepared exchanged branch.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-signed-data-and-entry",

        "Protected initial region",

      "method": "Rescale beam seeds, correct constraints, initialize exact gauge jets and propagate individual and odd exterior errors with one small common rate.",
      "output": "Nearby complete vacuum data and quadratic individual, cubic odd entry errors with exact early agreement.",
      "proof_anchor": "pm-h-24"
    },

Replace the amplitude in this section by $A_T=e^{-\\alpha T}$ and use Corollary <a href=\"#bg:amplitude-independent\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bg:amplitude-independent\">23</a>. For every fixed data order $m$, entry order $r$, loss $\\eta>0$ and late-support fraction $0<\\nu_0<1$, the conclusions of Proposition <a href=\"#prep:signed-entry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prep:signed-entry\">24</a> hold after choosing the frequency exponent sufficiently small and the inverse accuracy sufficiently large. This assertion ends at the entry cylinder.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-energy-volterra",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-normalized-coefficient-analysis"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-energy-volterra",
          "exact_quote": "For levels $0,\\ldots,K$ satisfying <a href=\"#energy:hierarchy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"energy:hierarchy\">[energy:hierarchy]</a>, with fixed $K$, there is a subexponential $P_T$, uniform for all $0\\le s\\le t\\le L$, such that $$\\label{energy:propagator}\n \\sum_{j\\le K}E_j(t)\n \\le P_T\\left\\{\n e^{\\mathcal R_T(t)-\\mathcal R_T(s)}\\sum_{j\\le K}E_j(s)\n +\\int_s^t e^{\\mathcal R_T(t)-\\mathcal R_T(\\tau)}\n                       \\sum_{j\\le K}S_j(\\tau)\\,d\\tau\\right\\}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-volterra-interval-propagator",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite energy hierarchy",
        "Nonnegative common rate",
        "Subexponential lower-level couplings"
      ],
      "method": "Conjugate by the common integrated rate and terminate the time-ordered expansion using strict commutation-level descents.",
      "output": "A subexponential propagator uniform in both time endpoints.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-energy-volterra"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-energy-signed-experiment",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-signed-data-and-entry",
        "step-normalized-coefficient-analysis",
        "step-volterra-interval-propagator"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-energy-signed-experiment",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\delta<\\kappa/32$ and the prescribed finite data seminorm. Use Proposition <a href=\"#bg:packet\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet\">21</a> and Proposition <a href=\"#prep:signed-entry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prep:signed-entry\">24</a> with sufficiently high fixed orders, sufficiently small losses and late support parameter, and sufficiently accurate linear residuals. For sufficiently small fixed $\\zeta>0$ and sufficiently large $T$, the two entry data have smooth vacuum evolutions $g_T^\\pm$ on the strict domain <a href=\"#energy:domain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"energy:domain\">[energy:domain]</a>, in wave-map gauge with target $g$. Let $H_T^\\pm$ be the relative arrays of $g_T^\\pm-g$, and put $$F_T^\\pm=H_T^\\pm\\mp q_T,\\qquad\n F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}=F_T^+-F_T^-.$$ Then $$\\label{energy:conclusion}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\lVert F_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^{30}_{\\mathrm{mix}}(\\Omega_T)}\n   &\\le e^{(\\kappa-2\\alpha+\\delta)T},\\\\\n \\lVert F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}(\\Omega_T)}\n   &\\le e^{(\\kappa-3\\alpha+\\delta)T}\n            +e^{(2\\kappa-4\\alpha+\\delta)T}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The exact changes and all three error fields have support in $v\\ge(1-\\nu_0)T-o(1)$. The time slices remain spacelike and the two moving side faces remain strict future exits. Moreover, for some $c>0$, $$\\label{energy:small-relative}\n \\lVert H_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^{30}_{\\mathrm{mix}}}\n       \\le e^{-(\\kappa/2+c)T},\\qquad\n e^{o(T)}\\left(\\lVert F_T^{\\mathrm{odd}}\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}}\n          +\\sum_\\pm\\lVert H_T^\\pm\\rVert_{C^2_{\\mathrm{mix}}}^2\\right)\n       =o(A_T).$$ Here every subexponential factor is at a fixed finite order. No higher seminorm of the fixed smooth background is required to be small.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-attach-development",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-interpolation-moments**: The displayed interpolation weights reproduce the quadratic monomial coefficient and annihilate the other monomial coefficients through the stated interpolation degree.
- **invariant-polynomial-extraction**: For a polynomial within the interpolation degree, the weighted samples recover its quadratic coefficient exactly after rescaling by the square of the positive sample spacing.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-polynomial-check-as-spacetime-certificate**: Treat exact interpolation checks as certification of generic future inextendibility. -> The finite arithmetic component does not establish the vacuum evolution, observer construction, loop estimates, or Baire genericity argument.
- **anti-ignore-interpolation-remainder**: Apply the polynomial extraction output to an arbitrary smooth function without accounting for its remainder. -> Sample values alone do not determine the quadratic coefficient for the full smooth-function regime; the source retains an integral remainder and a derivative bound.
- **anti-curvature-alone**: Use curvature growth alone to reject a future extension at the stated regularity. -> Continuous connection coefficients need not imply bounded classical curvature, so curvature growth does not supply the required transport contradiction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite constructive component is the exact interpolation-weight calculation and its polynomial specialisation, rather than the generic spacetime theorem. The source proves: “Both constants are finite and independent of $F,h,T$.” Its proof derives exact monomial moment identities by Lagrange interpolation. The executable domain checks those identities and exact quadratic extraction for rational polynomials of degree at most 17. The smooth-function remainder, geometric estimates, and residual-set conclusion remain outside this certificate.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with coefficients in ascending degree order and sample spacing h, all rational strings.
# PRECONDITIONS: Nonempty coefficient list of length at most 18; h is positive; object has exactly the declared keys.
# OUTPUTS: JSON object containing the exact rational interpolation weights and recovered quadratic coefficient.
# TIME: Fixed finite rational arithmetic; cost depends on rational operand sizes.
# SPACE: Fixed finite arrays of rational values; storage depends on rational operand sizes.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic throughout.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction
from math import comb

obj = json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(obj, dict) or set(obj) != {'coefficients', 'h'}:
    raise ValueError('invalid input object')
raw = obj['coefficients']
if not isinstance(raw, list) or not 1 <= len(raw) <= 18:
    raise ValueError('invalid coefficient list')
if not all(isinstance(x, str) for x in raw) or not isinstance(obj['h'], str):
    raise ValueError('rational strings required')
coefficients = [Fraction(x) for x in raw]
h = Fraction(obj['h'])
if h <= 0:
    raise ValueError('positive spacing required')
s1 = sum((Fraction(1, r) for r in range(1, 18)), Fraction(0))
s2 = sum((Fraction(1, r * r) for r in range(1, 18)), Fraction(0))
weights = [(s1 * s1 - s2) / 2]
for j in range(1, 18):
    weights.append(Fraction((-1) ** j * comb(17, j), j) * (s1 - Fraction(1, j)))
values = []
for j in range(18):
    x = j * h
    value = Fraction(0)
    for coefficient in reversed(coefficients):
        value = value * x + coefficient
    values.append(value)
quadratic = sum((weights[j] * values[j] for j in range(18)), Fraction(0)) / (h * h)
print(json.dumps({'weights': [str(x) for x in weights], 'quadratic': str(quadratic)}, sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\n\ndef verify():\n    if len(sys.argv) != 4:\n        return False\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        obj = json.load(stream)\n    if not isinstance(obj, dict) or set(obj) != {'coefficients', 'h'}:\n        return False\n    raw = obj['coefficients']\n    if not isinstance(raw, list) or not 1 <= len(raw) <= 18:\n        return False\n    if not all(isinstance(x, str) for x in raw) or not isinstance(obj['h'], str):\n        return False\n    coefficients = [Fraction(x) for x in raw]\n    if Fraction(obj['h']) <= 0:\n        return False\n    with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        answer = json.load(stream)\n    if not isinstance(answer, dict) or set(answer) != {'weights', 'quadratic'}:\n        return False\n    raw_weights = answer['weights']\n    if not isinstance(raw_weights, list) or len(raw_weights) != 18:\n        return False\n    if not all(isinstance(x, str) for x in raw_weights) or not isinstance(answer['quadratic'], str):\n        return False\n    # Independently solve the monomial moment equations, without the candidate's weight formula.\n    matrix = [[Fraction(j ** k) for j in range(18)] + [Fraction(int(k == 2))] for k in range(18)]\n    for column in range(18):\n        pivot = next(row for row in range(column, 18) if matrix[row][column] != 0)\n        matrix[column], matrix[pivot] = matrix[pivot], matrix[column]\n        divisor = matrix[column][column]\n        matrix[column] = [value / divisor for value in matrix[column]]\n        for row in range(18):\n            if row == column:\n                continue\n            factor = matrix[row][column]\n            if factor:\n                matrix[row] = [left - factor * right for left, right in zip(matrix[row], matrix[column])]\n    independent_weights = [matrix[j][-1] for j in range(18)]\n    if [Fraction(x) for x in raw_weights] != independent_weights:\n        return False\n    # The independent extraction reference reads the polynomial coefficient directly.\n    direct_coefficient = coefficients[2] if len(coefficients) > 2 else Fraction(0)\n    return Fraction(answer['quadratic']) == direct_coefficient == Fraction(sys.argv[3])\n\n\ntry:\n    accepted = verify()\nexcept Exception:\n    accepted = False\nsys.exit(0 if accepted else 1)\n",

The smooth-function remainder, geometric estimates, and residual-set conclusion remain outside this certificate.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with coefficients in ascending degree order and sample spacing h, all rational strings.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Nonempty coefficient list of length at most 18; h is positive; object has exactly the declared keys.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON object containing the exact rational interpolation weights and recovered quadratic coefficient.\n# TIME: Fixed finite rational arithmetic; cost depends on rational operand sizes.\n# SPACE: Fixed finite arrays of rational values; storage depends on rational operand sizes.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic throughout.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\nfrom math import comb\n\nobj = json.load(sys.stdin)\nif not isinstance(obj, dict) or set(obj) != {'coefficients', 'h'}:\n    raise ValueError('invalid input object')\nraw = obj['coefficients']\nif not isinstance(raw, list) or not 1 <= len(raw) <= 18:\n    raise ValueError('invalid coefficient list')\nif not all(isinstance(x, str) for x in raw) or not isinstance(obj['h'], str):\n    raise ValueError('rational strings required')\ncoefficients = [Fraction(x) for x in raw]\nh = Fraction(obj['h'])\nif h <= 0:\n    raise ValueError('positive spacing required')\ns1 = sum((Fraction(1, r) for r in range(1, 18)), Fraction(0))\ns2 = sum((Fraction(1, r * r) for r in range(1, 18)), Fraction(0))\nweights = [(s1 * s1 - s2) / 2]\nfor j in range(1, 18):\n    weights.append(Fraction((-1) ** j * comb(17, j), j) * (s1 - Fraction(1, j)))\nvalues = []\nfor j in range(18):\n    x = j * h\n    value = Fraction(0)\n    for coefficient in reversed(coefficients):\n        value = value * x + coefficient\n    values.append(value)\nquadratic = sum((weights[j] * values[j] for j in range(18)), Fraction(0)) / (h * h)\nprint(json.dumps({'weights': [str(x) for x in weights], 'quadratic': str(quadratic)}, sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The finite interpolation coefficients give a concrete software component for extracting the quadratic coefficient of a polynomial from sampled values. For a general smooth function, the same component returns an estimate with a remainder bound only when a certified derivative bound is supplied separately. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The constructed experiments are smooth vacuum spacetime evolutions with exact gauge and constraint conditions. The paper supplies no discrete scene evolution or simulation update rule. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The explicit interpolation construction supports an exact coefficient-checking tool. Such a tool can validate the polynomial moment identities and compute the finite constants used in a curvature-extraction estimate without claiming to certify the geometric inputs. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The interpolation lemma gives an explicit finite algorithm: compute the prescribed coefficients, combine the sampled values, and bound the remainder using the supplied derivative bound. Its polynomial extraction is exact; its smooth-function guarantee remains conditional on the analytic hypotheses. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The canonical state coordinates and transverse symplectic forms describe smooth phase spaces. They do not define a discrete representation with storage operations or data-structure guarantees. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The packet construction controls fixed finite derivative orders and analytic frequency losses, but supplies no hardware execution model, parallel decomposition or accelerator implementation. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The transport tests obstruct geometric spacetime extensions. They provide neither an adversarial security model nor a cryptographic construction; the ambient freedom concerns Lorentzian metrics. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The residual set is defined in a weighted smooth space of vacuum constraint data. The theorem gives no learning procedure or probability distribution for selecting members of that set. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The common support-preserving constraint inverse and contraction argument construct vacuum corrections. They do not minimize an objective or provide an operations-research feasibility algorithm for finite inputs. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Whole-bridge attachment joins smooth vacuum regions by actual collar isometries and proves a Cauchy property. Those geometric identifications do not specify communication, replicated state or distributed agreement. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The principal application is analytical general relativity: a residual subset of nearby complete vacuum data has future inextendible maximal developments. This existential genericity conclusion does not supply a finite engineering procedure for recognizing admissible data or residual-set membership. |

          "anchor": "test:section-form",
          "exact_quote": "Here is the exact volume statement used to transfer these bounds. On $T^*\\mathcal P_d$ take the canonical symplectic form $\\omega=d\\pi_\\alpha\\wedge dx^\\alpha$, with the corresponding Hamiltonian-flow sign convention, and set $\\mathcal H=\\tfrac12g^{-1}(\\pi,\\pi)$. Restrict to the future unit mass shell $\\mathcal H=-\\tfrac12$. Above a fixed $\\mathcal N_j$ the six variables $$(Y_1,\\theta^1,\\theta^2,\\pi_{Y_1},P_1,P_2)$$ give coordinates on the transverse section of this shell. To check the missing momentum explicitly, let $\\widetilde a=a/(\\kappa^2Y_0Y_1)>0$ and $\\widetilde b=-b/(\\kappa Y_0)$. Then $$g=-2\\widetilde a\\,dY_0dY_1+\n \\gamma_{AB}(d\\theta^A-\\widetilde b^A dY_0)\n             (d\\theta^B-\\widetilde b^B dY_0),$$ and the shell equation is $$\\label{test:mass-shell-solve}\n -\\widetilde a^{-1}\n       (\\pi_{Y_0}+\\widetilde b^AP_A)\\pi_{Y_1}\n       +\\tfrac12\\gamma^{AB}P_AP_B=-\\tfrac12.$$ Future timelikeness gives $\\dot Y_0<0$ and hence $\\pi_{Y_1}=-\\widetilde a\\dot Y_0>0$. Thus Equation <a href=\"#test:mass-shell-solve\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"test:mass-shell-solve\">[test:mass-shell-solve]</a> determines $\\pi_{Y_0}$ uniquely. Pulling back $\\omega$ to this section, where $dY_0=0$, gives the nondegenerate form $$\\label{test:section-form}\n \\omega_j=d\\pi_{Y_1}\\wedge dY_1\n              +\\sum_{A=1}^2dP_A\\wedge d\\theta^A.$$ Its absolute symplectic volume is ordinary canonical coordinate volume; there is no factor involving $a$ or $Y_0$.",
          "source_object": "test:section-form"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-bg-packet",
          "exact_quote": "Fix any finite list of output derivative orders, including an exterior base buffer $N_b$. For every fixed $\\zeta>0$ and every subsequently prescribed inverse accuracy $M_1$, sufficiently high finite transport and Taylor orders yield a real interior tensor $h_T^p$ and a real exterior tensor $h_T^B$ with the following properties. Put $$\\label{bg:packet-scales}\n       A_T=e^{-\\alpha T},\\qquad \\alpha=7\\kappa/8,\n       \\qquad\\lambda_T=e^{\\zeta T}.$$ The interior field has the form $$\\label{bg:packet-form}\n h_T^p=A_T\\operatorname{tr.rev.}_g\\operatorname{Re}\n       \\left(e^{i\\lambda_Tv}\\sum_{j=0}^{J}\\lambda_T^{-j}p_j\\right).$$ Every fixed normalized coefficient jet of $p_j$ is $e^{o(T)}$. The field is supported in a fixed-width neighborhood of $v=T$, with angular support in the transported launch patch. Its relative array $q_T=(h_T^p(\\hat e_\\alpha,\\hat e_\\beta))$ satisfies $$\\label{bg:packet-size}\n              \\|q_T\\|_{C^N(\\mathcal D_T)}\n                    \\le A_T\\lambda_T^N e^{o(T)}$$ at each specified order. The tensor $p_0$ is trace-free and $p_0(L_0,\\cdot)=0$. There is a purely angular $\\gamma$-unit vector $X$ at $p_T$ for which $$\\label{bg:polarization}\n \\sigma_T:=\\left|\\operatorname{Re}\n        \\bigl(e^{i\\lambda_TT}p_0(X,X)\\bigr)\\right|$$ has two-sided subexponential size. The $a$-scaled linear wave residual and $\\sqrt a$-scaled linear gauge residual, in normalized slots and with all prescribed ordinary derivatives, are bounded by $A_T\\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$.*\n\n\n*In regular exterior lab components, $$\\label{bg:beam-estimates}\n \\|h_T^B\\|_{C^{N_b}}\\le A_T\\lambda_T^{N_b+3/2}e^{o(T)}.$$ Its linear wave and gauge residuals, and its difference from $h_T^p$ on the entry collar, have size $A_T\\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$ at all the prescribed finite orders. The latter assertion includes normal spacetime jets. On the original bridge its support lies in Equation <a href=\"#bg:arrival-shell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:arrival-shell\">[bg:arrival-shell]</a> with arbitrarily thin tube margins. Increasing $M_1$ and the finite formal degrees does not increase the positive frequency powers in Equations <a href=\"#bg:packet-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:packet-size\">[bg:packet-size]</a> and <a href=\"#bg:beam-estimates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"bg:beam-estimates\">[bg:beam-estimates]</a>. Thresholds and subexponential constants may depend on those degrees.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-rotating-subextremal-center** (hypothesis): The theorem concerns a fixed positive-mass rotating subextremal Kerr center. It does not cover the nonrotating or extremal endpoints. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem to nonrotating or extremal reference data.', 'Assume estimates uniform as the reference center approaches an excluded endpoint.']
- **limit-local-weighted-vacuum-neighborhood** (hypothesis): Admissible initial data are smooth, positive, complete vacuum constraint data on the prescribed bridge, with finite weighted seminorms and the stated neighborhood smallness. Blocked use: ['Apply the result to matter-coupled initial data.', 'Apply the result to arbitrary large perturbations or incomplete initial surfaces.', 'Replace the weighted smooth topology by an unspecified numerical norm.']
- **limit-residual-not-universal** (formal_scope): The conclusion holds on a dense residual subset of the admissible neighborhood. It does not assert that every nearby datum is inextendible or that inextendibility is open. Blocked use: ['Certify an individual nearby datum solely from neighborhood membership.', 'Treat inextendibility as an open property proved by this theorem.']
- **limit-category-not-probability** (formal_scope): The genericity conclusion uses Baire category in the weighted smooth constraint space; it supplies no sampling distribution or probabilistic guarantee for arbitrary initial data. Blocked use: ['Interpret residual genericity as an almost-sure statement under an unspecified probability law.', 'Infer residual-set membership from randomly sampled finite observations.']
- **limit-specified-future-extension** (formal_scope): The obstruction concerns the defined future timelike exit into an ambient spacetime with a nondegenerate differentiable Lorentzian metric. The ambient spacetime may be nonvacuum and need not be globally hyperbolic. Blocked use: ['Claim exclusion of every merely continuous extension from this theorem.', 'Restrict the theorem to vacuum ambient extensions.', 'Claim a past-extension theorem from the future-exit result.']
- **limit-curvature-alone-insufficient** (formal_scope): Curvature growth alone does not obstruct the specified extension regularity. The proof requires the parallel-transport contradiction. Blocked use: ['Replace the holonomy argument by a curvature-growth observation without establishing a transport obstruction.']
- **limit-transport-test-full-quantification** (hypothesis): A passing test requires the common duration and developed-length transport bounds for every seed, positive observer time and admissible loop specified in its definition. Blocked use: ['Infer that a datum passes from finitely many sampled seeds or loops.', 'Replace developed length by coordinate length.', 'Use loops outside the required future region.']
- **limit-chart-bootstrap-preconditions** (hypothesis): The local loop bound requires common bounds on the connection, chart margins, prescribed frames and their inverses. Blocked use: ['Apply the uniform loop estimate without a positive chart margin.', 'Assume frame comparison remains uniform for an uncontrolled family of observers.']
- **limit-comparison-runout-not-boundary** (formal_scope): Branch and corner runout describe coordinate limits in an auxiliary comparison development. They do not identify it with the full maximal development or adjoin spacetime boundary points. Blocked use: ['Treat comparison runout as the full-development endpoint.', 'Assume a regular ambient endpoint for the selected branch observer.']
- **limit-track-dependent-corner-bounds** (hypothesis): The optical-speed and screen-velocity bounds depend on the individual track's initial tangent. Corner nullity does not supply a uniform momentum bound over all seeds. Blocked use: ["Use one track's constants as uniform bounds for an arbitrary open family.", 'Conclude that every seed avoids the corner regime.']
- **limit-fixed-order-subexponential-control** (approximation): Subexponential bounds permit dependence on fixed derivative and formal expansion orders. They do not provide uniform estimates over unbounded orders or computational complexity guarantees. Blocked use: ['Let derivative orders increase with observation depth while retaining the stated bounds.', 'Interpret analytic growth bounds as runtime or storage bounds.']
- **limit-packet-choice-order** (hypothesis): Finite output and buffer orders must precede the frequency choice, which precedes inverse accuracy and formal degrees. Increasing formal accuracy preserves the already fixed positive frequency powers, but may change thresholds and constants. Blocked use: ['Choose frequency before fixing all derivative requirements.', 'Assume a single frequency exponent provides convergence at every derivative order simultaneously.']
- **limit-common-constraint-inverse-and-tails** (hypothesis): The signed constraint correction requires small base norms, compact exterior defects and the same support-preserving linear inverse for both signs. The physical corrections retain their decaying exterior tails. Blocked use: ['Use unrelated correction operators and retain the claimed odd cancellation.', 'Apply the contraction estimate outside its small base-norm regime.', 'Discard the correction tail while claiming the resulting datum is the constructed exact vacuum datum.']
- **limit-entry-jets-and-common-gauge** (hypothesis): Exterior preparation controls full spacetime entry jets, including normal derivatives, in exact wave-map gauge with a common target and exact early agreement. Blocked use: ['Replace full entry matching by agreement of tangential values alone.', 'Transfer the signed estimates between independently chosen gauges without establishing the required identification.']
- **limit-general-amplitude-stops-at-entry** (formal_scope): The general-amplitude exterior corollary ends at the entry cylinder. It does not extend the signed interior experiment to arbitrary amplitude decay rates. Blocked use: ['Use exterior amplitude independence as a proof of arbitrary-amplitude interior propagation or holonomy separation.']
- **limit-intermediate-time-signed-propagation** (hypothesis): The odd estimate requires a common growth rate preserved on every intermediate interval and higher mass buffers. An individual error need not be smaller than the packet. Blocked use: ['Bound each source using a fresh terminal growth factor and retain the claimed odd gain.', 'Assume individual packet domination instead of checking the odd and quadratic remainder bounds.']
- **limit-whole-data-attachment** (hypothesis): The observation must belong to a development of the entire corrected datum, with actual matching collars, preserved tails and a proved Cauchy property before the maximal-development embedding is invoked. Blocked use: ['Embed a partial experiment by maximality without proving whole-surface Cauchyness.', 'Substitute approximate packet matching for exact solution identifications.', 'Continue past causal curves across omitted artificial faces.']
- **limit-absolute-shell-and-observer-control** (hypothesis): The shell estimate controls absolute canonical momentum on the fixed tube within the actual constructed domain. Returning the adaptive observer additionally uses the stronger relative smallness needed for terminal conversion. Blocked use: ['Divide the root error by the vanishing lapse and claim relative root closeness.', 'Extend the perturbed vector field beyond its constructed domain to justify backward tracking.', 'Treat the stronger observer estimate as an unnecessary gluing assumption.']
- **limit-loop-flow-and-boost-identities** (hypothesis): The loop bounds use actual parameter-dependent flows, exact null identities and controlled boosted connection jets in an attached future observation box. Blocked use: ['Replace the parameter-dependent flow by an unverified composition of separate flows.', 'Estimate developed length solely from coordinate displacement.', 'Discard potentially growing boosted entries without proving their exact vanishing or perturbative control.']
- **limit-interpolation-remainder** (approximation): Exact polynomial extraction is a finite component of the argument. For general smooth functions, the identity retains its integral remainder and requires the stated derivative bound for the resulting inequality. Blocked use: ["Infer an arbitrary smooth function's quadratic coefficient exactly from finite samples.", 'Claim a certified remainder from sample values without independently bounding the required derivative.', 'Treat polynomial arithmetic checks as certification of the spacetime theorem.']
- **limit-no-finite-residual-membership-procedure** (nonconstructive): The residual set is obtained by a countable intersection in an infinite-dimensional smooth constraint space. The supplied proof does not provide a finite decision procedure for membership of an arbitrary datum. Blocked use: ['Turn finite successful experiments into a complete decision procedure for generic inextendibility.', 'Report a finite interpolation certificate as a certificate for all geometric and analytic stages.']
- **limit-spatial-isometries-not-spacetime-symmetry** (formal_scope): The appendix breaks spatial data isometries using hypersurfaces in Kerr. These examples do not remove Kerr spacetime Killing fields and are separate from the signed genericity construction. Blocked use: ['Infer absence of spacetime Killing fields from trivial spatial data isometry group.', "Use the appendix's normal-graph examples as the test-destroying perturbations."]

          "exact_quote": "Fix any finite list of output derivative orders, including an exterior base buffer $N_b$. Its relative array $q_T=(h_T^p(\\hat e_\\alpha,\\hat e_\\beta))$ satisfies $$\\label{bg:packet-size}\n              \\|q_T\\|_{C^N(\\mathcal D_T)}\n                    \\le A_T\\lambda_T^N e^{o(T)}$$ at each specified order. There is a purely angular $\\gamma$-unit vector $X$ at $p_T$ for which $$\\label{bg:polarization}\n \\sigma_T:=\\left|\\operatorname{Re}\n        \\bigl(e^{i\\lambda_TT}p_0(X,X)\\bigr)\\right|$$ has two-sided subexponential size. The $a$-scaled linear wave residual and $\\sqrt a$-scaled linear gauge residual, in normalized slots and with all prescribed ordinary derivatives, are bounded by $A_T\\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$.*\n\n\n*In regular exterior lab components, $$\\label{bg:beam-estimates}\n \\|h_T^B\\|_{C^{N_b}}\\le A_T\\lambda_T^{N_b+3/2}e^{o(T)}.$$ Its linear wave and gauge residuals, and its difference from $h_T^p$ on the entry collar, have size $A_T\\lambda_T^{-M_1}e^{o(T)}$ at all the prescribed finite orders. The latter assertion includes normal spacetime jets.           "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\delta<\\kappa/32$ and the prescribed finite data seminorm. The full physical constraint-correction tail is part of its initial data.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-attach-geometric-contract",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For each fixed Kerr spacetime with mass <i>M</i> &gt; 0 and rotation $`0\lt \mathfrak a\lt M`$, we prove that a dense <i>G</i><sub><i>δ</i></sub> set of nearby smooth, complete two-ended vacuum data has a maximal globally hyperbolic development with no future <i>C</i><sup>1</sup> extension. The neighborhood and genericity are defined in a weighted smooth topology, and ambient extensions may be nonvacuum.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Einstein vacuum equations determine spacetime from initial geometry only as long as the evolution remains a Cauchy development. Fourès-Bruhat proved local existence and geometric uniqueness (Fourès-Bruhat 1952) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[ChoquetBruhat1952](#ref-ChoquetBruhat1952)]; Choquet-Bruhat and Geroch established a maximal globally hyperbolic development, unique up to an isometry preserving the initial data (Choquet-Bruhat and Geroch 1969) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ChoquetBruhatGeroch1969](#ref-ChoquetBruhatGeroch1969)]. An extension beyond that development need not be determined by the same data. Strong cosmic censorship asks whether such extensions are excluded for generic initial data in an appropriate regularity class. Penrose’s discussion of the strong formulation relates this question to global hyperbolicity and to the infinite blueshift at an inner horizon (Penrose 1979, sec. 12.3.2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Penrose1979](#ref-Penrose1979)].

Kerr’s rotating vacuum solutions (Kerr 1963) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Kerr1963](#ref-Kerr1963)] make this question particularly sharp. Their subextremal interiors contain Cauchy horizons, so the exact solutions admit continuations beyond their maximal Cauchy developments (Carter 1968) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Carter1968](#ref-Carter1968)]. The conjectural mechanism is instability under perturbation. Here we prove a local, Baire-generic $C^1$ form of future strong cosmic censorship near each fixed rotating subextremal Kerr bridge. The data are smooth, complete, two-ended vacuum data with no symmetry condition. Genericity is measured in a weighted smooth topology; the neighborhood requires smallness of only its tenth seminorm. The precise theorem and the definition of a future timelike exit appear in Section <a href="#sec:phase" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:phase">2</a>. The neighborhood depends on the Kerr center, and the conclusion concerns every such exit of the full maximal globally hyperbolic development, allowing nonvacuum ambient metrics.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Why extension regularity matters

The physical analyses of Poisson and Israel (Poisson and Israel 1990) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[PoissonIsrael1990](#ref-PoissonIsrael1990)] and Ori (Ori 1991, 1992) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Ori1991](#ref-Ori1991), [Ori1992](#ref-Ori1992)] describe how blueshift can produce mass inflation and a weak tidal singularity at an inner horizon. Rigorous nonlinear work in the spherically symmetric Einstein–Maxwell–scalar-field model established that continuous extendibility can coexist with instability of curvature and mass (Dafermos 2003, 2005) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Dafermos2003](#ref-Dafermos2003), [Dafermos2005](#ref-Dafermos2005)]. In that model, Luk and Oh proved a generic $C^2$ inextendibility theorem for admissible complete two-ended asymptotically flat data, combining exterior lower bounds with an interior instability argument (Luk and Oh 2019a, 2019b) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[LukOh2019I](#ref-LukOh2019I), [LukOh2019II](#ref-LukOh2019II)]. Building on their work, Sbierski (Sbierski 2022, Theorem 4.31) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Sbierski2022](#ref-Sbierski2022)] proved future locally Lipschitz inextendibility for generic sufficiently small spherically symmetric perturbations of two-ended subextremal Reissner–Nordström data in the same matter model, allowing extensions that do not preserve spherical symmetry. The matter model, spherical symmetry and topology in those results are part of their statements.

For the vacuum equations without symmetry, Luk (Luk 2018) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Luk2018](#ref-Luk2018)] constructed stable local weak null singularities from singular characteristic data: in the constructed coordinates the metric extends continuously while its Christoffel symbols fail to be locally square integrable. Dafermos and Luk (Dafermos and Luk 2025) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[DafermosLuk2025](#ref-DafermosLuk2025)] prove persistence of a continuously extendible Cauchy-horizon portion from suitable Cauchy data already inside a black hole. Hintz (Hintz 2026, Theorem 13.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Hintz2026](#ref-Hintz2026)] proves nonlinear exterior stability throughout the subextremal range for small data with specified finite asymptotic power–log expansions and a faster decaying remainder. Combined with the interior theorem of Dafermos and Luk, this gives $C^0$ Cauchy-horizon stability for that data class. Its asymptotic assumptions differ from the symbol-bounded tails allowed in our weighted phase space.

Gurriaran (Gurriaran 2026, Theorem 4.5.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Gurriaran2026](#ref-Gurriaran2026)] obtains locally Lipschitz inextendibility near timelike infinity from interior spacelike data with prescribed nonlinear Price asymptotics and a nonvanishing nonaxisymmetric leading curvature amplitude. Luk and Sbierski (Luk and Sbierski 2026, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LukSbierski2026](#ref-LukSbierski2026)] obtain continuous extendibility and locally Lipschitz inextendibility across a specified Cauchy-horizon portion from characteristic event-horizon data satisfying quantitative upper and lower radiation bounds. These results explain the interior instability mechanisms and the importance of the incoming radiation. The theorem here addresses a different data question: a residual subset of a neighborhood of complete vacuum Cauchy data, without assuming a radiation lower bound, and with every future exit covered.

A $C^1$ metric has continuous connection coefficients but need not have bounded classical curvature. Curvature growth alone therefore does not obstruct a $C^1$ extension; our argument uses parallel transport. Sbierski’s holonomy method (Sbierski 2022) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Sbierski2022](#ref-Sbierski2022)] supplies a direct geometric precedent; his later criterion (Sbierski 2026, Theorem 3.14) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Sbierski2026](#ref-Sbierski2026)] excludes locally Lipschitz extensions across the specified weak-null boundary under stable signed integrated-curvature blowup and the specified geometric hypotheses. Our proof constructs finite loops for which the transport bound forced by a $C^1$ extension fails. It proves the required quantitative estimates within the complete Cauchy evolution.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The foundation and the two-sign construction

The companion (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)] supplies the complete near-Kerr background evolution, exterior constraint inverse, and constrained linear packet construction. We state the particular geometric and linear conclusions in Sections <a href="#test:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:section">3</a> and <a href="#sec:background" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:background">4</a>, including their data hypotheses, derivative orders and parameter dependence. The proofs below use those intermediate results; the companion’s final $C^2$ inextendibility conclusion is not a prerequisite.

The companion (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[KerrWeakConnection](#ref-KerrWeakConnection)] excludes continuous nondegenerate future extensions with locally square-integrable connection for a local residual set near each fixed rotating subextremal Kerr bridge. That stronger inextendibility conclusion builds on the intermediate packet, constraint, and geometric results developed here, together with its own nonlinear comparison and averaging over families of loops. The present paper proves the pointwise transport obstruction at $C^1$ regularity; the square-integrable-connection theorem is not an input to its proof.

The constraint step belongs to the development of localized vacuum deformations initiated by Corvino, Corvino–Schoen and Chruściel–Delay (Corvino 2000; Corvino and Schoen 2006; Chruściel and Delay 2003) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Corvino2000](#ref-Corvino2000), [CorvinoSchoen2006](#ref-CorvinoSchoen2006), [ChruscielDelay2003](#ref-ChruscielDelay2003)]. Here an inner ball must remain exactly unchanged while a decaying exterior correction is retained. This makes the early evolution identical and leaves room for a late experiment. Complex Gaussian beams and their superpositions allow an oscillatory packet to cross real caustics (Ralston 1982; Tanushev 2008; Liu, Runborg, and Tanushev 2013) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Ralston1982](#ref-Ralston1982), [Tanushev2008](#ref-Tanushev2008), [LiuRunborgTanushev2013](#ref-LiuRunborgTanushev2013)]. The present construction returns that packet to the original initial surface and controls the Einstein wave-gauge constraints, matching of normal jets and an interval whose length grows with the observation depth; those estimates are proved in the companion and in Section <a href="#sec:background" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:background">4</a>.

The analytic difficulty is that a packet capable of producing the needed signal need not dominate its own nonlinear comparison error. We prepare two exact vacuum perturbations with opposite linear parts, using the same constraint inverse and the same background gauge. Their pure-packet quadratic forcing agrees. Subtracting the two evolution equations cancels that forcing and gives an estimate for the difference of the two errors. When the curvature observations are then subtracted, the background term cancels and the remaining quadratic curvature terms are small enough to control. One of the two observations must therefore be large. Section <a href="#prep:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:section">5</a> establishes the signed cancellation at the entry cylinder, and Section <a href="#energy:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="energy:section">6</a> propagates it through the interior. The energy estimate keeps one common growth rate at every fixed derivative order and on every intermediate time interval; this is what allows the subtraction to preserve its gain even when an individual error exceeds the linear packet.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### From one future exit to one finite contradiction

The logical starting point is a hypothetical future $C^1$ exit. In a regular extension chart, bounded connection coefficients give a linear bound for the holonomy of loops of small developed length. Here developed length means the length of the loop’s velocity measured in a frame transported along that same loop. Section <a href="#test:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="test:section">3</a> shows that one bound holds along an open family of finite-duration timelike geodesics. These requirements form countably many closed tests on the initial data. It is enough to destroy each test by an arbitrarily small perturbation in any prescribed finite seminorm.

The comparison development is an auxiliary Cauchy development that contains the entire initial bridge and an interior region described by two increasing optical coordinates. Its two branch regimes have one coordinate tending to infinity and the other to a finite limit; in the corner regime both coordinates diverge. These are coordinate limits, with no boundary points adjoined to the perturbed spacetime. A passing test supplies a geodesic approaching one of the two noncorner branches of the comparison development. The reason is geometric: the geodesic flow preserves canonical volume on transverse sections of the unit mass shell, while corner tracks eventually occupy strips of arbitrarily small such volume. An open family of initial seeds cannot consist entirely of corner tracks. This argument does not impose a bound on curvature along the selected observer.

At a late finite point of that branch, the signed packet construction produces the curvature separation just described. Section <a href="#attach:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="attach:section">7</a> attaches both experiments to developments of the entire corrected initial surface, retaining their asymptotic tails. It also chooses nearby observing seeds that reach the prescribed observation point. The comparison solves the perturbed unit mass shell for an absolute canonical momentum; it avoids dividing an error by the vanishing optical lapse.

Finally, Section <a href="#holo:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="holo:section">8</a> rescales a null frame adapted to that observer. Exact null identities control the potentially growing connection entries. The resulting loop family has exceptionally short developed length and controlled finite derivatives of its holonomy with respect to loop size. A finite interpolation formula extracts the quadratic holonomy coefficient, which is curvature. The assumed linear transport bound makes this coefficient smaller than the signed signal. The relevant scales can already be seen here. At optical depth $T$, the lapse satisfies $a_p\asymp e^{-\kappa T}$ for a fixed $\kappa>0$. We choose packet amplitude $A_T=e^{-7\kappa T/8}$ and loop parameter $h_T=e^{-\kappa T/16}$. The signed construction gives a scaled curvature component larger than $A_T$, whereas the transport test and finite interpolation would give the bound $$e^{o(T)}(a_p/h_T+h_T^{16})
 =e^{o(T)}\bigl(O(e^{-15\kappa T/16})+e^{-\kappa T}\bigr)
 =o(A_T).$$ The factor $e^{o(T)}$ represents losses smaller than every fixed positive exponential rate, at the finite derivative orders in use. This strict separation is the finite contradiction that destroys the test. Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">9</a> then applies the Baire theorem and checks the order of all choices. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-map-signed-tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-map-signed-tests">1</a> records this route through the proof.

<figure id="fig:proof-map-signed-tests">

<figcaption>The route from a possible future exit to a finite contradiction. Each experiment is attached to the full development of its complete corrected datum. The noncorner branch locates an observation; the final contradiction concerns parallel transport in the full development.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="sec:phase"></a>
### The constraint space and the theorem

We use Lorentzian signature $(-+++)$. Fix $M>0$ and $0<\mathfrak a<M$, where $\mathfrak a$ denotes the Kerr rotation parameter. The letter $a$ will later denote a double-null metric coefficient, unrelated to this parameter.

Let $(\Sigma,h_*,K_*)$, $\Sigma\simeq\mathbb R\times\mathbb S^2$, be the complete signed Killing-time-zero Kerr bridge through the bifurcation sphere, with the future-normal convention of (OpenAI 2026b, companion@kind@foundation@sec:introduction companion@kind@foundation@sec:introduction ) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. Fix its identification with $\Sigma$, asymptotically Cartesian charts on both ends, and a smooth function $\rho\ge1$ equal to the end radius outside a compact set. Write $\nabla_*$ and $|\cdot|_*$ for the reference connection and tensor norm.

<a id="stmt-phase-space"></a>
<div id="phase:space" class="definition">

<a id="phase:seminorms"></a>
<a id="phase:constraints"></a>
**Definition 1** (Weighted constraint space). For smooth symmetric two-tensors $f,j$ set $$\label{phase:seminorms}
 p_m(f,j)=\sum_{i=0}^m
 \left\{\sup_\Sigma \rho^{1+i}|\nabla_*^if|_*
       +\sup_\Sigma \rho^{2+i}|\nabla_*^ij|_*\right\},
 \qquad m=0,1,\ldots.$$ Let $\mathcal D$ consist of smooth pairs $d=(h,K)$ such that $h$ is positive definite and complete, every $p_m(h-h_*,K-K_*)$ is finite, and $$\label{phase:constraints}
 R(h)+(\operatorname{tr}_hK)^2-|K|_h^2=0,\qquad
 \operatorname{div}_hK-\mathrm d(\operatorname{tr}_hK)=0.$$ The topology on $\mathcal D$ is the relative topology of the seminorms <a href="#phase:seminorms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="phase:seminorms">[phase:seminorms]</a>. For $\varepsilon>0$ let $$\mathcal U_\varepsilon=\{d\in\mathcal D:p_{10}(d-d_*)<\varepsilon\},
 \qquad d_*=(h_*,K_*).$$

</div>

These are differentiated $O(r^{-1})$ and $O(r^{-2})$ end conditions. There is no parity, fixed leading coefficient, fixed charge, or symmetry restriction. In particular, we do not require every possible asymptotic charge integral to exist for every allowed tail. We use representatives in the fixed spatial gauge, rather than a quotient by diffeomorphisms.

The choice of equivalent end charts, weights and reference geometry does not change the topology. More precisely, suppose the radii are comparable, the transition Jacobian and its inverse are symbols of order zero, and the transformed reference geometry has the same asymptotic class. The tensor chain rule and the formula for changing connections bound each seminorm in one choice by a finite sum of seminorms in the other, in both directions. To retain the Cartesian convention, the leading Euclidean metric is preserved up to the allowed metric-weight error. A smooth reidentification carrying both the data and the reference center therefore gives a homeomorphism. This assertion does not identify seminorms under arbitrary data-dependent gauge transformations.

For $d\in\mathcal D$, let $\mathcal M_d$ denote its full smooth maximal globally hyperbolic vacuum development, with the prescribed time orientation. We use smooth Cauchy existence, propagation of the wave-gauge constraints, geometric uniqueness, and compact-subset Cauchy stability in their usual smooth-data form; the required local estimates are part of the Cauchy framework in (OpenAI 2026b, companion@kind@foundation@sec:category companion@kind@foundation@sec:category ) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Foundation](#ref-Foundation)]. The defining embedding property of the maximal development is (Sbierski 2016, Definitions 2.1–2.2 and Theorems 2.6–2.8) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Sbierski2016](#ref-Sbierski2016)]. An application of that property below will always be preceded by a proof that the attached spacetime has the *entire* initial manifold as a Cauchy surface.

<a id="stmt-phase-extension"></a>
<div id="phase:extension" class="definition">

**Definition 2** (Future $C^1$ extension). A future $C^1$ extension of $\mathcal M_d$ is a smooth, time-orientation-preserving isometric embedding $\iota:\mathcal M_d\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal M}$ onto a proper open subset of a connected, time-oriented smooth four-manifold with nondegenerate $C^1$ Lorentzian metric $\widetilde g$, together with a future-directed timelike $C^1$ curve $c:[0,1]\to\widetilde{\mathcal M}$ such that $$c([0,1))\subset\iota(\mathcal M_d),\qquad
 c(1)\in\partial\iota(\mathcal M_d).$$ Neither the vacuum equations nor global hyperbolicity are imposed on the ambient spacetime.

</div>

<a id="stmt-phase-main"></a>
<div id="phase:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3**. *For each fixed $M>0$ and $0<\mathfrak a<M$, there is $\varepsilon_0=\varepsilon_0(M,\mathfrak a)>0$ such that $\mathcal U_{\varepsilon_0}$ contains a dense $G_\delta$ subset $\mathcal G$ for which every $\mathcal M_d$, $d\in\mathcal G$, has no future $C^1$ extension in the sense of Definition <a href="#phase:extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="phase:extension">2</a>.*

</div>

The open neighborhood and the residual subset in this statement are different sets. The theorem does not assert that inextendibility is open, or that every datum in $\mathcal U_{\varepsilon_0}$ has the conclusion. Its constants need not be uniform as $\mathfrak a\to0$ or $\mathfrak a\to M$. All later choices of the base neighborhood use only the smallness of $p_{10}$; higher seminorms are finite but need not be uniformly small. Appendix <a href="#phase:symmetry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="phase:symmetry">10</a> constructs nearby data with trivial spatial data isometry group and explains how nearby Kerr centers fit into the same phase space. These observations are separate from the genericity argument.

<a id="stmt-phase-baire"></a>
<div id="phase:baire" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Baire property). *The space $\mathcal D$ is completely metrizable, and every $\mathcal U_\varepsilon$ is a nonempty Baire space. At any point, finite seminorm balls form a neighborhood base: every neighborhood contains a set of the form $$\{d'\in\mathcal D:p_m(d'-d)<r\}$$ for some finite $m\ge10$ and $r>0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-phase-baire"><summary>Proof of stmt-phase-baire</summary>

*Proof.* The vector space $\mathcal E$ of tensor pairs with every seminorm <a href="#phase:seminorms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="phase:seminorms">[phase:seminorms]</a> finite is complete for the usual metric built from the countable increasing seminorms. Indeed, a Cauchy sequence converges smoothly on compact sets, and its weighted uniform derivative limits agree with the derivatives of that local smooth limit. Passing the uniform Cauchy bounds to the limit gives convergence in each $p_m$.

For any $h=h_*+f$ with $h>0$ and $p_0(f,0)<\infty$, the asymptotic decay makes $h$ uniformly comparable with $h_*$ on the ends. Positivity on the remaining compact set gives the same comparison there. Thus $h$ is complete. The same positive lower comparison bound shows that positivity is open in $\mathcal E$. Consequently the completeness requirement in Definition <a href="#phase:space" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="phase:space">1</a> is automatic on this positive open set.

The constraint map is continuous there into the space of smooth scalar and one-form fields with the local smooth topology. Its zero locus is relatively closed. An open subset of a completely metrizable space is completely metrizable, as is a closed subset of it. This proves the assertion for $\mathcal D$, and then for its open subsets $\mathcal U_\varepsilon$. They contain $d_*$.
